\Gamma_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k],\omega_{1},q^{\prime}}\sim 2\gamma\Big(2\gamma\sigma_{\varsigma}[k]-(1+\rho[k])\Big)|v|^{2},

so that
\Gamma_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k],\omega_{1},q^{\prime}}\to-\infty
if and only if

\frac{\sigma_{\varsigma}[k]}{1+\rho[k]}<\frac{1}{2\gamma}.

Since
\gamma\in(0,1/2)
we can write

\frac{1}{2\gamma}=1+\kappa_{\omega_{1}}

for some
\kappa_{\omega_{1}}>0
. Next, we can prove that if
c_{\omega_{1}}
is sufficiently small, then
c\leq c_{\omega_{1}}
implies

\frac{\sigma_{\varsigma}[k]}{1+\rho[k]}\leq 1+\frac{\kappa_{\omega_{1}}}{2}<\frac{1}{2\gamma},

because
\sigma_{\varsigma}[k]\sim 1
and
1+\rho[k]\sim 1
, independently of
\varsigma
, if
k
is small in
\|\cdot\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}
.
∎

Lemma 4.3
.

Let
M,R>1
, and suppose that
\omega_{1}\in\mathcal{W}_{\mu^{-1/2}}
is given. Also we let
k\in\mathcal{X}_{\omega,c}
with
c\leq\min\{c_{1},c_{\omega_{1}}\}
, where
c_{\omega_{1}}>0
is as in Lemma
4.2
. Consider any weight
\widetilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
satisfying
\widetilde{\omega}\omega_{1}^{-1}\in L^{2}\cap L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then for any
b_{T}\in L^{2}_{\widetilde{\omega}^{-1}}
, the dual backwards problem (
4.9
) associated to
\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]^{*}
admits a unique solution in
b\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{\widetilde{\omega}^{-1}}(\mathcal{O}))
. As a result, for any
q\in[1,2]
we also have
b\in C([0,T];L^{q}_{\omega_{1}^{-1}}(\mathcal{O}))
. Furthermore, there exist
M_{\omega_{1}},R_{\omega_{1}}>1
sufficiently large (and independent of
\varsigma
or the exponent
q
) such that for all

M\geq M_{\omega_{1}},\quad R\geq R_{\omega_{1}},

the dissipative estimate below holds for some constant
\lambda^{\prime}>0
:

-\frac{\textnormal{d}}{\textnormal{d}t}\frac{1}{q}\iint_{\mathcal{O}}|b\omega_{1}^{-1}|^{q}+4\frac{(q-1)}{q^{2}}\iint_{\mathcal{O}}\sigma_{\varsigma}[k]|\nabla_{v}(b\omega_{1}^{-1})^{q/2}|^{2}\leq-\lambda^{\prime}\iint_{\mathcal{O}}|b\omega_{1}^{-1}|^{q}.

In particular

(4.10)

\|b_{t}\|_{L^{q}_{\omega_{1}^{-1}}(\mathcal{O})}\leq e^{-\lambda^{\prime}(T-t)}\|b_{T}\|_{L^{q}_{\omega_{1}^{-1}}(\mathcal{O})}\quad\forall t\in[0,T],\quad\forall q\in[1,2].

Proof
. We can easily check that the embedding
L^{2}_{\widetilde{\omega}^{-1}}\hookrightarrow L^{q}_{\omega_{1}^{-1}}
is continuous. Thus, the well-posedness of (
4.9
) in
C([0,T];L^{q}_{\omega_{1}^{-1}})
is an immediate consequence of Proposition
2.1
.

It now suffices to prove the estimate (
4.10
), under the assumption that
c_{\omega_{1}}>0
is small enough and
M,R
are large enough. One has (for instance by using the renormalized Green’s formula)

(4.11)

\begin{split}&-\frac{\textnormal{d}}{\textnormal{d}t}\frac{1}{q}\iint_{\mathcal{O}}|b_{t}|^{q}\omega_{1}^{-q}\\
&=\iint_{\mathcal{O}}|b_{t}|^{q-2}b_{t}(\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]^{*}b_{t})\omega_{1}^{-q}\\
&=\iint_{\mathcal{O}}|b_{t}|^{q-2}b_{t}\Big[(\mathsf{L}_{\varsigma}[k]^{*}-M\psi_{R})b_{t}\Big]\omega_{1}^{-q}\\
&=\iint_{\mathcal{O}}\Big(\Gamma_{\mathsf{L}_{\varsigma}[k]^{*},\omega_{1}^{-1},q}-M\psi_{R}\Big)|b_{t}|^{q}\omega_{1}^{-q}-\frac{4(q-1)}{q^{2}}\iint_{\mathcal{O}}\sigma_{\varsigma}[k]|\nabla_{v}(b_{t}\omega_{1}^{-1})^{q/2}|^{2},\end{split}

where we used (
2.2
) and the fact that no boundary term appears thanks to the specular reflection boundary condition. Now

\Gamma_{\mathsf{L}_{\varsigma}[k]^{*},\omega_{1}^{-1},q}=\Gamma_{\mathsf{L}_{\varsigma}[k],\omega_{1},q^{\prime}},

where
1/q^{\prime}+1/q=1
. Using Lemma
4.2
, we choose
c_{\omega_{1}}>0
small enough so that
c\leq\min\{c_{1},c_{\omega_{1}}\}
and
k\in\mathcal{X}_{\omega,c}
implies

\sup_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\Omega}\sup_{q^{\prime}\in[2,\infty]}\limsup_{|v|\to\infty}\Gamma_{\mathsf{L}_{\varsigma}[k],\omega_{1},q^{\prime}}(v)\leq-1/2,

and thus for all sufficiently large
M,R>1
, it holds that

\sup_{q\in[1,2]}\Big(\Gamma_{\mathsf{L}_{\varsigma}[k],\omega_{1},q^{\prime}}(v)-M\psi_{R}(v)\Big)\leq-1/4\quad\text{in }\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathcal{O}.

Going back to (
4.11
) and then applying Grönwall’s inequality, we arrive at (
4.10
).
∎

Using the backwards equation, we can now extend the
L^{2}
results of Lemma
4.1
to a general
L^{p}_{\omega}
-framework. We will only consider the case of large
M,R
, since that is enough for our purposes later.

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
p\in[2,\infty]
,
\omega_{1}\in\mathcal{W}_{\mu^{-1/2}}
, and suppose
M\geq M_{\omega_{1}},R\geq R_{\omega_{1}}
. We further assume that
c\leq\min\{c_{1},c_{\omega_{1}}\}
and
k\in\mathcal{X}_{\omega,c}
(see the notations of Lemmas
4.2
–
4.3
). Then for given
h_{0}\in L^{p}_{\omega_{1}}(\mathcal{O})
, for the linear problem (
4.7
) associated to
\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]
, its unique solution in
h\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{\widetilde{\omega}}(\mathcal{O}))
(see Lemma
4.1
) satisfies

\|h_{T}\|_{L^{p}_{\omega_{1}}}\leq e^{-\lambda^{\prime}T}\|h_{0}\|_{L^{p}_{\omega_{1}}}\quad\forall T>0.

Here
\lambda^{\prime}>0
is given as in Lemma
4.3
. Furthermore, the solution lies in
h\in C([0,T];L^{p}_{\omega_{1}}(\mathcal{O}))
if
p\in[2,\infty)
, and
C([0,T];L^{\infty}_{\omega_{1}}(\mathcal{O})-*)
if
p=\infty
; thus the linear problem (
4.7
) is well-posed in these spaces. We may therefore associate to it an evolution system (or non-autonomous semigroup) which we denote
(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,s))_{0\leq s\leq t}
. This family satisfies the following dissipative estimate:

(4.12)

\forall h_{s}\in L^{p}_{\omega_{1}},\quad\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,s)h_{s}\|_{L^{p}_{\omega_{1}}}\leq e^{-\lambda^{\prime}(t-s)}\|h_{s}\|_{L^{p}_{\omega_{1}}}.

Proof
. Since
\widetilde{\omega}\omega_{1}^{-1}\in L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we remind the reader that
h_{0}\in L^{p}_{\omega_{1}}(\mathcal{O})\subset L^{2}_{\widetilde{\omega}}(\mathcal{O})
. For any given
b_{T}\in L^{2}_{\widetilde{\omega}^{-1}}
, we may consider the unique solution
b\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{\widetilde{\omega}^{-1}})
to the backwards dual problem (
4.9
) associated to
\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]^{*}
(see Lemma
4.3
). Owing to Lemma
2.3
, we have the following duality result

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{O}}h_{T}b_{T}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v=\iint_{\mathcal{O}}h_{0}b(0)\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v.

Consequently, by using that
L^{2}_{\widetilde{\omega}^{-1}}
is dense in
L^{p^{\prime}}_{\omega^{-1}}
, we can compute the
L^{p}_{\omega}
-norm of
h_{T}
as follows:

\displaystyle\|h_{T}\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{T}\in L^{2}_{\widetilde{\omega}^{-1}},\\
\|b_{T}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{\omega^{-1}}}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\iint_{\mathcal{O}}h_{T}b_{T}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v

\displaystyle=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{T}\in L^{2}_{\widetilde{\omega}^{-1}},\\
\|b_{T}\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}_{\omega^{-1}}}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\iint_{\mathcal{O}}h_{0}b(0)\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v

\displaystyle\leq e^{-\lambda^{\prime}T}\|h_{0}\|_{L^{p}_{\omega}}.

It follows that
h_{T}\in L^{p}_{\omega}
for every
T\geq 0
. The claim that
t\mapsto h_{t}\in L^{p}_{\omega}
(or
L^{\infty}_{\omega}-*
) is continuous just follows as a consequence of the duality formula; we refer to the proof of Proposition
3.6
. Finally, the general result (
4.12
) follows by a time translation and it is thus skipped.
∎

4.4.
Ultracontractivity property of the dissipative operator
\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]

In this section we aim to prove an ultracontractivity estimate for the solution to (
4.7
) associated to
\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]
. The arguments of this section follow in large what has already been demonstrated in
[
11
]
. As before, most of the analysis will be performed on the backwards problem (
4.9
), and we will transition later by resorting to the duality argument in Lemma
2.3
. We stress that all of the estimates here hold
independently of
\varsigma
, as those in Section
4.3
.

The starting step is to obtain a boundary penalizing estimate for
b
. The following lemma, inspired from
[
10
]
, exploits a suitable twisted weight which allows us to obtain the penalized estimate, while killing the troublesome boundary contributions at the same time. The weight utilized here takes a simpler form due to the fact that we only incorporate the specular reflection boundary condition, contrary to
[
10
,
11
]
where the Maxwell boundary condition was considered.

Lemma 4.5
.

We let
\mathfrak{m}:(0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be such that
\mathfrak{m}^{-1}\in\mathcal{W}_{\mu^{-1/2}}
and
\mathfrak{m}^{-1}
is not a polynomial weight (in other words
\mathfrak{m}^{-1}\in\mathcal{W}_{\mu^{-1/2}}
and
\mathfrak{m}^{-1}=\langle v\rangle^{m}e^{\gamma|v|^{\tau}}
with
\gamma>0
,
\tau>0
). Let
b\in C([0,T];L^{2}_{\mathfrak{m}^{-1}}(\mathcal{O}))
be the solution to the dual equation (
4.9
) associated to
\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]^{*}
as constructed under the setting of Lemma
4.3
. Then, for
c\leq\min\{c_{1},c_{\mathfrak{m}^{-1}}\}
and
k\in\mathcal{X}_{\omega,c}
, it holds for all
M,R
large enough (chosen dependently on
\mathfrak{m}^{-1}
) that for any
0\leq\varphi\in\mathcal{D}((0,T))
and
\alpha\in(0,1)
:

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}b_{t}^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\frac{(n_{x}\cdot v)^{2}}{\langle v\rangle^{2}\mathfrak{d}(x)^{1-\alpha}}\varphi^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|b\mathfrak{m}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}^{2}\varphi^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}b_{t}^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\varphi(\varphi^{\prime})_{-}.

Here
\overline{\mathfrak{m}}:\mathcal{O}\to(0,\infty)
is some twisted weight function satisfying
\mathfrak{m}\lesssim\overline{\mathfrak{m}}\lesssim\mathfrak{m}
, to be specified in the proof, and
c_{\mathfrak{m}^{-1}}
is the constant as in the third statement of Lemma
4.2
, with weight
\omega_{1}=\mathfrak{m}^{-1}
.

Proof of Lemma
4.5
.
For any
\chi\in\mathcal{D}(\overline{\mathcal{O}})
, the solution
b
satisfies

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\textnormal{d}}{\textnormal{d}t}\iint_{\mathcal{O}}b_{t}^{2}\chi\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v

\displaystyle=\iint_{\mathcal{O}}b_{t}(\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]^{*}b_{t})\chi\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v+\frac{1}{2}\iint_{\partial\mathcal{O}}b_{t}^{2}\chi(v\cdot n_{x})\,\textnormal{d}\sigma_{x}\textnormal{d}v,

or equivalently

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\textnormal{d}}{\textnormal{d}t}\iint_{\mathcal{O}}b_{t}^{2}\chi\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\iint_{\mathcal{O}}b_{t}^{2}(v\cdot\nabla_{x}\chi)\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v+\iint_{\mathcal{O}}b_{t}(\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]^{*}b_{t})\chi\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\iint_{\partial\mathcal{O}}\gamma b_{t}^{2}\chi(v\cdot n_{x})\,\textnormal{d}\sigma_{x}\textnormal{d}v.

Therefore, for any
0\leq\varphi\in\mathcal{D}((0,T))
we can do a simple integration by parts to obtain

(4.13)

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}b_{t}^{2}\chi\varphi\varphi^{\prime}\\
&=-\frac{1}{2}\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}b_{t}^{2}(v\cdot\nabla_{x}\chi)\varphi^{2}+\iint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}b_{t}(\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]^{*}b_{t})\chi\varphi^{2}\\
&\quad+\frac{1}{2}\iiint_{(0,T)\times\partial\mathcal{O}}\gamma b_{t}^{2}\chi\varphi^{2}(v\cdot n_{x}).\end{split}

We choose the following specific test function

\chi=\overline{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}:=\mathfrak{m}^{2}\left(1-\frac{\mathfrak{d}(x)^{\alpha}}{4D^{\alpha}}\frac{n_{x}\cdot v}{\langle v\rangle^{2}}\right),

which obviously satisfies the relations
\mathfrak{m}\lesssim\overline{\mathfrak{m}}\lesssim\mathfrak{m}
. With this choice, let us explicitly compute the three terms on the right-hand side of (
4.13
). For the first term, we have

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}b_{t}^{2}(v\cdot\nabla_{x}\chi)\varphi^{2}

\displaystyle=\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}b_{t}^{2}\frac{\mathfrak{m}^{2}}{8D^{\alpha}\langle v\rangle^{2}}v\cdot\nabla_{x}\Big(\mathfrak{d}(x)^{\alpha}(n_{x}\cdot v)\Big)\varphi^{2}

\displaystyle=\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}b_{t}^{2}\frac{\mathfrak{m}^{2}}{8D^{\alpha}\langle v\rangle^{2}}v\cdot\Big(\alpha\mathfrak{d}(x)^{\alpha-1}\nabla_{x}\mathfrak{d}(x)\,(n_{x}\cdot v)+\mathfrak{d}(x)^{\alpha}\nabla_{x}n_{x}\,v\Big)\varphi^{2}

\displaystyle=\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}b_{t}^{2}\frac{\mathfrak{m}^{2}}{8D^{\alpha}\langle v\rangle^{2}}\Big(-\alpha\mathfrak{d}(x)^{\alpha-1}|\nabla_{x}\mathfrak{d}(x)|(n_{x}\cdot v)^{2}+\mathfrak{d}(x)^{\alpha}v\cdot\nabla_{x}n_{x}\,v\Big)\varphi^{2},

where we recalled that
\nabla_{x}\mathfrak{d}(x)=-|\nabla_{x}\mathfrak{d}(x)|n_{x}
. For the second term, we may use (
2.2
) to obtain

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}b_{t}(\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]^{*}b_{t})\overline{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\varphi^{2}

\displaystyle=\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}\Gamma_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]^{*},\overline{\mathfrak{m}},2}|b_{t}|^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\varphi^{2}-\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}\sigma_{\varsigma}[k]|\nabla_{v}(b\overline{\mathfrak{m}})|^{2}\varphi^{2}.

Finally, using that
\mathfrak{d}(x)=0
for
x\in\partial\Omega
and also the (dual) specular reflection boundary condition, we get

\displaystyle\iiint_{(0,T)\times\partial\mathcal{O}}(\gamma b_{t})^{2}\chi\varphi^{2}

\displaystyle=\iiint_{(0,T)\times\partial\mathcal{O}}(\gamma b_{t})^{2}\mathfrak{m}^{2}\varphi^{2}=0.

Collecting everything, (
4.13
) is now equivalent to

(4.14)

\begin{split}&\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}b_{t}^{2}\mathfrak{m}^{2}\frac{\alpha}{8D^{\alpha}\langle v\rangle^{2}\mathfrak{d}(x)^{1-\alpha}}|\nabla_{x}\mathfrak{d}(x)|(n_{x}\cdot v)^{2}\varphi^{2}+\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}\sigma_{\varsigma}[k]|\nabla_{v}(b\overline{\mathfrak{m}})|^{2}\varphi^{2}\\
&=-\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}b_{t}^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\left(1-\frac{\mathfrak{d}^{\alpha}}{4D^{\alpha}}\frac{n_{x}\cdot v}{\langle v\rangle^{2}}\right)\varphi\varphi^{\prime}\\
&\quad+\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}b_{t}^{2}\mathfrak{m}^{2}\frac{\mathfrak{d}(x)^{\alpha}v\cdot\nabla_{x}n_{x}\,v}{4D^{\alpha}\langle v\rangle^{2}}\varphi^{2}+\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}\Gamma_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]^{*},\overline{\mathfrak{m}},2}|b_{t}|^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\varphi^{2}.\end{split}

In order to conclude, we observe that
\Gamma_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]^{*},\overline{\mathfrak{m}},2}=\Gamma_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k],\overline{\omega},2}
with
\overline{\omega}:=\overline{\mathfrak{m}}^{-1}
. Now
\overline{\mathfrak{m}}
and
\mathfrak{m}
have equivalent growth, so it is enough to compute
\Gamma_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k],\mathfrak{m}^{-1},2}
. Since
\mathfrak{m}^{-1}
is assumed not to be a polynomial weight, we can apply either the second or third statement of Lemma
4.2
to find that for
c\leq\min\{c_{1},c_{\mathfrak{m}^{-1}}\}
, it holds for all sufficiently large
M,R
that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k],\overline{\mathfrak{m}}^{-1},2}=\Gamma_{\mathsf{L}_{\varsigma}[k],\mathfrak{m}^{-1},2}-M\psi_{R}\leq-\frac{8}{3}\|\nabla_{x}n_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}}.

In particular, for such choices of
M,R
, the last line of (
4.14
) is strictly negative, and more specifically it satisfies

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}b_{t}^{2}\mathfrak{m}^{2}\frac{\mathfrak{d}(x)^{\alpha}v\cdot\nabla_{x}n_{x}\,v}{4D^{\alpha}\langle v\rangle^{2}}\varphi^{2}+\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}\Gamma_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]^{*},\overline{\mathfrak{m}},2}|b_{t}|^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\varphi^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-\|\nabla_{x}n_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}}\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}|b_{t}|^{2}\mathfrak{m}^{2}\varphi^{2}.

Consequently, from (
4.14
) we deduce

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}b_{t}^{2}\mathfrak{m}^{2}\frac{\alpha}{\langle v\rangle^{2}\mathfrak{d}(x)^{1-\alpha}}(n_{x}\cdot v)^{2}\varphi^{2}+\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}\Big[|b\mathfrak{m}|^{2}+\sigma_{\varsigma}[k]|\nabla_{v}(b\overline{\mathfrak{m}})|^{2}\Big]\varphi^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}b_{t}^{2}\overline{\mathfrak{m}}^{2}\varphi(\varphi^{\prime})_{-}.

The thesis of the lemma then follows by recalling that
\mathfrak{m}\lesssim\overline{\mathfrak{m}}\lesssim\mathfrak{m}
,
\sigma_{\varsigma}[k]\gtrsim 1
(see (
4.8
)), and also observing that

\displaystyle\lVert b\mathfrak{m}\rVert_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}\lesssim\lVert b\overline{\mathfrak{m}}\rVert_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}\lesssim\lVert b\mathfrak{m}\rVert_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}.

∎

We then recall the following useful interpolation estimate from
[
11
, Lemma 5.3]
.

Lemma 4.6
.

For any function
f:\mathcal{O}\to\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{d}^{-1/4}\langle v\rangle^{-1}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}\lesssim\iint_{\mathcal{O}}f^{2}\frac{(n_{x}\cdot v)^{2}}{\langle v\rangle^{2}\mathfrak{d}^{1/2}}+\|\nabla_{v}f\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}.

Applying this lemma with
f=b\overline{\mathfrak{m}}
, we deduce immediately that the assumptions of Lemma
4.5
imply (with the choice of
\alpha=1/2
)

(4.15)

\left\|b\overline{\mathfrak{m}}\mathfrak{d}^{-1/4}\langle v\rangle^{-1}\varphi\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}^{2}\lesssim\left\|b\overline{\mathfrak{m}}\sqrt{\varphi(\varphi^{\prime})_{-}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}^{2}.

In order to make use of (
4.15
), we mention a rather standard gain of integrability estimate which is local in nature.

Lemma 4.7
.

We adopt the setting and all of the assumptions of Lemma
4.5
. Let
b
be the solution to the dual equation (
4.9
) associated to
\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]^{*}
as constructed under the setting of Lemma
4.3
. Then it holds for each
p\in\left(2,\frac{2d+1}{d}\right)
and
p_{1}\in\left(p,\frac{2d+1}{d}\right)
that

\displaystyle\left\|b\mathfrak{m}\mathfrak{d}^{p_{1}/p}\langle v\rangle^{-2}\varphi\right\|_{L^{p}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}\lesssim C_{T}\left(\left\|b\mathfrak{m}\sqrt{\varphi(\varphi^{\prime})_{-}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}+\|b\mathfrak{m}\varphi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}\right),

\displaystyle C_{T}:=\left(T^{\frac{(2d+1)(p+2)}{2p}-2d}+T^{\frac{(2d+1)(p+2)}{2p}-2d-\frac{1}{2}}\right).

Proof
. Given
\nu(x)\in\mathcal{D}(\Omega)
, we denote
\mathfrak{m}_{0}:=\langle v\rangle^{-2}\mathfrak{m}
and
\mathfrak{b}:=b\mathfrak{m}_{0}\nu\varphi
. Then
\mathfrak{b}
is a solution to

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{x})\mathfrak{b}

\displaystyle=-\sigma_{\varsigma}[k]\Delta_{v}\mathfrak{b}-\sigma_{\varsigma}[k]\nu\varphi\Big(2\nabla_{v}b\cdot\nabla_{v}\mathfrak{m}_{0}+b\Delta_{v}\mathfrak{m}_{0}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\nabla_{v}\cdot(\zeta[k]\mathfrak{b})-\zeta[k]\cdot b\nabla_{v}\mathfrak{m}_{0}\,\nu\varphi

\displaystyle\quad-\eta[k]\mathfrak{b}+M\chi_{R}\mathfrak{b}+vb\mathfrak{m}_{0}\varphi\cdot\nabla_{x}\nu+b\mathfrak{m}_{0}\nu\varphi^{\prime}

in the sense of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}((0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
. The above can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\left(-\partial_{t}-v\cdot\nabla_{x}-\Delta_{v}\right)\mathfrak{b}

\displaystyle=T_{1}+\nabla_{v}\cdot T_{2},

where

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle:=-\sigma_{\varsigma}[k]\nu\varphi\Big(2\nabla_{v}b\cdot\nabla_{v}\mathfrak{m}_{0}+b\Delta_{v}\mathfrak{m}_{0}\Big)-\zeta[k]\cdot b\nu\varphi\nabla_{v}\mathfrak{m}_{0}

\displaystyle\qquad-(\eta[k]+M\chi_{R})\mathfrak{b}+vb\mathfrak{m}_{0}\varphi\cdot\nabla_{x}\nu+b\mathfrak{m}_{0}\nu\varphi^{\prime},

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle:=(\sigma_{\varsigma}[k]-1)\nabla_{v}\mathfrak{b}+\zeta[k]\mathfrak{b}.

We point out that since
\mathfrak{m}^{-1}\in\mathcal{W}
, it holds

\displaystyle|\nabla_{v}\mathfrak{m}|\lesssim\langle v\rangle\mathfrak{m},\quad|\nabla_{v}\mathfrak{m}_{0}|\lesssim\langle v\rangle^{-1}\mathfrak{m},\quad|\Delta_{v}\mathfrak{m}_{0}|\lesssim\mathfrak{m}.

Consequently, using that

\sigma_{\varsigma}[k]\lesssim 1,\quad\zeta[k]\lesssim\langle v\rangle,\quad\eta[k]\lesssim 1,

we obtain

\displaystyle|T_{1}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle v\rangle^{-2}|\nabla_{v}(b\mathfrak{m})|^{2}\varphi^{2}+b^{2}\mathfrak{m}^{2}\varphi^{2}+\langle v\rangle^{-2}b^{2}\mathfrak{m}^{2}\varphi^{2}|\nabla_{x}\nu|^{2}+\langle v\rangle^{-2}b^{2}\mathfrak{m}^{2}|\varphi^{\prime}|^{2}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle|T_{2}|^{2}\lesssim\langle v\rangle^{-4}|\nabla_{v}(b\mathfrak{m})|^{2}\varphi^{2}+\langle v\rangle^{-6}b^{2}\mathfrak{m}^{2}\varphi^{2}+\langle v\rangle^{-2}b^{2}\mathfrak{m}^{2}\varphi^{2}.

By estimating all terms of the form
\|b\mathfrak{m}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}^{2}\varphi^{2}
by using Lemma
4.5
, namely that

\int_{0}^{T}\|b\mathfrak{m}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{v}}^{2}\varphi^{2}\lesssim\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}b^{2}\mathfrak{m}^{2}\varphi(\varphi^{\prime})_{-},

we deduce (rather crudely)

\displaystyle\|T_{1}\|_{L^{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|T_{2}\|_{L^{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nu\|_{W_{x}^{1,\infty}(\Omega)}\left(\left\|b\mathfrak{m}\sqrt{\varphi(\varphi^{\prime})_{-}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}+\|b\mathfrak{m}\varphi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}\right).

The remaining parts of the proof are then completely the same as with
[
11
]
. We clean up the signs by setting
\mathfrak{B}(t,x,v)=\mathfrak{b}(T-t,x,-v)
and similarly
S_{i}(t,x,v)=T_{i}(T-t,x,-v)
,
i=1,2
. In this way,
\mathfrak{B}
solves

\displaystyle(\partial_{t}+v\cdot\nabla_{v}-\Delta_{v})\mathfrak{B}=S_{1}-\nabla_{v}\cdot S_{2}

in the sense of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}((0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and therefore admits the representation formula

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}(t,x,v)

\displaystyle=\iiint_{t,x,v}\mathfrak{K}(t-t^{\prime},x-x^{\prime}-(t-t^{\prime})v^{\prime},v-v^{\prime})(S_{1}(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},v^{\prime})-\nabla_{v^{\prime}}\cdot S_{2}(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},v^{\prime}))\,\textnormal{d}t^{\prime}\textnormal{d}x^{\prime}\textnormal{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\iiint_{t,x,v}\mathfrak{K}(t-t^{\prime},x-x^{\prime}-(t-t^{\prime})v^{\prime},v-v^{\prime})S_{1}(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},v^{\prime})\,\textnormal{d}t^{\prime}\textnormal{d}x^{\prime}\textnormal{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle\quad-\iiint_{t,x,v}\nabla_{v}\mathfrak{K}(t-t^{\prime},x-x^{\prime}-(t-t^{\prime})v^{\prime},v-v^{\prime})S_{2}(t^{\prime},x^{\prime},v^{\prime})\,\textnormal{d}t^{\prime}\textnormal{d}x^{\prime}\textnormal{d}v^{\prime},

where
\mathfrak{K}
is the fundamental solution of the Kolmogorov equation (see for instance
[
2
]
or the classical
[
35
]
). Namely, we have the explicit expression

\mathfrak{K}(t,x,v)=\frac{C_{0}}{t^{2d}}\exp\left(-\frac{3}{t^{3}}\left|x-\frac{t}{2}v\right|^{2}-\frac{1}{4t}|v|^{2}\right)

where
C_{0}
is a normalizing constant, and for any
q\in\left[1,\frac{4d+2}{4d+1}\right)
, a direct computation shows that

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{K}\|_{L^{q}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim T^{\frac{2d+1}{q}-2d},

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{v}\mathfrak{K}\|_{L^{q}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim T^{\frac{2d+1}{q}-2d-\frac{1}{2}}.

Hence the representation formula and Young’s inequality for convolutions show

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{B}\|_{L^{p}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathfrak{K}\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{p+2}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|S_{1}\|_{L^{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}+\|\nabla_{v}\mathfrak{K}\|_{L^{\frac{2p}{p+2}}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\|S_{2}\|_{L^{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{T}\|\nu\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{x}(\Omega)}\left(\left\|b\mathfrak{m}\sqrt{\varphi(\varphi^{\prime})_{-}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}+\|b\mathfrak{m}\varphi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}\right)

for every
p\in\left[2,\frac{2d+1}{d}\right)
.
Note that in the above, we made use of the fact that
\|S_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x,v}}=\|T_{i}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x,v}}
. In the same way, we have
\|\mathfrak{B}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x,v}}=\|\mathfrak{b}\|_{L^{p}_{t,x,v}}
, and so we have obtained

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{b}\|_{L^{p}([0,T]\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{T}\|\nu\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{x}(\Omega)}\left(\left\|b\mathfrak{m}\sqrt{\varphi(\varphi^{\prime})_{-}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}+\|b\mathfrak{m}\varphi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}\right).

Finally, we set
\Omega_{k}:=\left\{x\in\Omega\;\middle|\;\mathfrak{d}(x)>2^{-k}\right\}
and define
\mathcal{O}^{T}_{k}:=(0,T)\times\Omega_{k}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We choose the cutoffs
\nu_{k}\in\mathcal{D}(\Omega)
so that
\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{k+1}}\leq\nu_{k}\leq\mathbf{1}_{\Omega_{k}}
and
\|\nu_{k}\|_{W^{1,\infty}_{x}(\Omega)}\lesssim 2^{k}
for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
. From the previous arguments that we just established, we know that for any
p\in\left[1,\frac{2d+1}{d}\right)
, there holds

\displaystyle\|b\mathfrak{m}_{0}\varphi\|_{L^{p}(\mathcal{O}_{k}^{T})}\lesssim C_{T}2^{k}\left(\left\|b\mathfrak{m}\sqrt{\varphi(\varphi^{\prime})_{-}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}+\|b\mathfrak{m}\varphi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}\right),

\displaystyle C_{T}:=T^{\frac{2d+1}{q}-2d}+T^{\frac{2d+1}{q}-2d-\frac{1}{2}}.

Then using that
\mathfrak{d}\sim 2^{-k}
on each
\mathcal{O}^{T}_{k}
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}}\mathfrak{d}^{p_{1}}|b\mathfrak{m}_{0}\varphi|^{p}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}_{k+1}\setminus\mathcal{O}^{T}_{k}}\mathfrak{d}^{p_{1}}|b\mathfrak{m}_{0}\varphi|^{p}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}2^{-kp_{1}}\iiint_{\mathcal{O}^{T}_{k+1}\setminus\mathcal{O}^{T}_{k}}|b\mathfrak{m}_{0}\varphi|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}^{p}\left(\left\|b\mathfrak{m}\sqrt{\varphi(\varphi^{\prime})_{-}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}+\|b\mathfrak{m}\varphi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}\right)^{p}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}2^{k(p-p_{1})+1}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{T}^{p}\left(\left\|b\mathfrak{m}\sqrt{\varphi(\varphi^{\prime})_{-}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}+\|b\mathfrak{m}\varphi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}\right)^{p},

which finishes the proof.
∎

Combining the result of Lemma
4.7
with what we mentioned in (
4.15
), we obtain an up-to-the-boundary
L^{2}\to L^{r}
estimate for some
r>2
.

Lemma 4.8
.

We adopt the setting and all of the assumptions of Lemma
4.5
, along with the notations of Lemma
4.7
. Then there exists an exponent
r\in(2,p)
such that

(4.16)

\|b\mathfrak{m}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\theta}\varphi\|_{L^{r}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}\lesssim C_{T}^{1-\theta}\left(\left\|b\mathfrak{m}\sqrt{\varphi(\varphi^{\prime})_{-}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}+\|b\mathfrak{m}\varphi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}\right),

where

\theta:=\frac{p_{1}}{p}\left(\frac{p_{1}}{p}+\frac{1}{4}\right)^{-1}\in(0,1).

Proof
. Using Hölder’s inequality, we immediately obtain

\|b\mathfrak{m}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\theta}\varphi\|_{L^{r}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}\leq\|b\mathfrak{m}\delta^{-1/4}\langle v\rangle^{-1}\varphi\|^{\theta}_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}\|b\mathfrak{m}\delta^{p_{1}/p}\langle v\rangle^{-2}\varphi\|^{1-\theta}_{L^{p}(\mathcal{O}^{T})},

where

\frac{1}{r}=\frac{\theta}{2}+\frac{1-\theta}{p}.

Thanks to (
4.15
) and Lemma
4.7
, we further obtain that

\|b\mathfrak{m}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\theta}\varphi\|_{L^{r}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}\lesssim C_{T}^{1-\theta}A_{1}^{\theta}(A_{1}+A_{2})^{1-\theta},

where
A_{1}=\left\|b\mathfrak{m}\sqrt{\varphi(\varphi^{\prime})_{-}}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}
and
A_{2}=\|b\mathfrak{m}\varphi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}
. Since
(A_{1}+A_{2})^{1-\theta}\leq A_{1}^{1-\theta}+A_{2}^{1-\theta}
, we get

\displaystyle\|b\mathfrak{m}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\theta}\varphi\|_{L^{r}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{T}^{1-\theta}(A_{1}+A_{1}^{\theta}A_{2}^{1-\theta})

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{T}^{1-\theta}(A_{1}+A_{2}),

that last inequality following due to Young’s inequality. This concludes the proof.
∎

At last, we may establish the
L^{1}\to L^{2}
estimate for the solution to the backwards equation, which is the cornerstone of De Giorgi’s arguments.

Lemma 4.9
.

We adopt the same assumptions and notations of Lemmas
4.5
–
4.7
–
4.8
. Suppose that
T\in(0,1]
, and that the terminal datum
b_{T}
associated to the dual problem (
4.9
) satisfies
b_{T}\in L^{1}_{\mathfrak{m}_{1}}(\mathcal{O})
, with

\mathfrak{m}_{1}:=\mathfrak{m}\langle v\rangle^{2-\theta}.

Then whenever
c\leq\min\{c_{1},c_{\mathfrak{m}^{-1}},c_{\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{-1}}\}
and
M,R
are made larger as necessary (so that in particular
M\geq M_{\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{-1}}
and
R\geq R_{\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{-1}}
), there exists a constant
\vartheta>0
such that

\displaystyle\|b_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{m}}(\mathcal{O})}\lesssim\frac{1}{T^{\vartheta}}\|b_{T}\|_{L^{1}_{\mathfrak{m}_{1}}(\mathcal{O})}.

Proof
. In order to transition the result of Lemma
4.8
into an
L^{1}\to L^{2}
estimate, we use Hölder’s inequality to write each term of the right-hand side of (
4.16
) as

\displaystyle\|b\mathfrak{m}\sqrt{\varphi(\varphi^{\prime})_{-}}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\left\|b\mathfrak{m}\langle v\rangle^{2-\theta}(\varphi^{\prime}/\varphi)^{1/2\Lambda}\varphi\right\|_{L^{1}}^{\Lambda}\|b\mathfrak{m}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\theta}\varphi\|_{L^{r}}^{1-\Lambda},

\displaystyle\|b\mathfrak{m}\varphi^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\left\|b\mathfrak{m}\langle v\rangle^{2-\theta}(\varphi^{\prime}/\varphi)^{1/\Lambda}\varphi\right\|^{\Lambda}_{L^{1}}\|b\mathfrak{m}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\theta}\varphi\|_{L^{r}}^{1-\Lambda},

where
\Lambda=\frac{r-2}{2(r-1)}\in(0,1)
. Inserting these bounds into the right-hand side of (
4.16
) and then dividing both sides by
\|b\mathfrak{m}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\theta}\varphi\|_{L^{r}}^{1-\Lambda}
, we obtain

(4.17)

\|b\mathfrak{m}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\theta}\varphi\|_{L^{r}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}\lesssim C_{T}^{\frac{1-\theta}{\Lambda}}\left(\left\|b\mathfrak{m}\langle v\rangle^{2-\theta}(\varphi^{\prime}/\varphi)^{\frac{1}{2\Lambda}}\varphi\right\|_{L^{1}}+\left\|b\mathfrak{m}\langle v\rangle^{2-\theta}(\varphi^{\prime}/\varphi)^{\frac{1}{\Lambda}}\varphi\right\|_{L^{1}}\right),

in other words an
L^{1}\to L^{r}
estimate.

For the sake of simplicity, let us now set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}_{1}:=\mathfrak{m}\langle v\rangle^{2-\theta},\quad\mathfrak{m}_{2}:=\mathfrak{m}\langle v\rangle^{-2+\theta},

so that (
4.17
) is nothing but

(4.18)

\begin{split}&\|b\mathfrak{m}_{2}\varphi\|_{L^{r}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}\lesssim C_{T}^{\frac{1-\theta}{\Lambda}}(B_{1}+B_{2}),\\
&B_{1}:=\left\|b\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\varphi^{\prime}/\varphi)^{\frac{1}{2\Lambda}}\varphi\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O}^{T})},\quad B_{2}:=\left\|b\mathfrak{m}_{1}(\varphi^{\prime}/\varphi)^{\frac{1}{\Lambda}}\varphi\right\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O}^{T})}.\end{split}

Furthermore, let us fix
0\leq\varphi_{0}\in\mathcal{D}((0,1))
and define
\varphi\in\mathcal{D}((0,T))
explicitly as
\varphi(t):=\varphi_{0}(t/T)
. Then using
L^{p}
-interpolation, we can obtain the following estimate for the
L^{2}
-norm:

(4.19)

\begin{split}\|\varphi_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{2}\|b_{0}{\bf m}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}&=T\int_{0}^{T}(\varphi^{\prime})^{2}\|b_{0}{\bf m}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}\\
&\leq T\int_{0}^{T}(\varphi^{\prime})^{2}\|b_{t}\mathfrak{m}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}\\
&\leq T\int_{0}^{T}(\varphi^{\prime})^{2}\|b_{t}\mathfrak{m}_{1}\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}^{2\Lambda}\|b_{t}\mathfrak{m}_{2}\|_{L^{r}}^{2(1-\Lambda)}\\
&=T\int_{0}^{T}\Big[(\varphi^{\prime}/\varphi)^{2}\varphi^{2\Lambda}\|b_{t}\mathfrak{m}_{1}\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}^{2\Lambda}\Big]\Big[\varphi^{\frac{r}{r-1}}\|b_{t}\mathfrak{m}_{2}\|_{L^{r}}^{2(1-\Lambda)}\Big]\\
&\leq T\left(\int_{0}^{T}(\varphi^{\prime}/\varphi)^{\frac{1}{\Lambda}}\varphi\|b_{t}\mathfrak{m}_{1}\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}\right)^{\frac{r-2}{r-1}}\left(\int_{0}^{T}\varphi^{r}\|b_{t}\mathfrak{m}_{2}\|^{r}_{L^{r}}\right)^{\frac{1}{r-1}}\\
&\leq TC_{T}^{\frac{2r(1-\theta)}{r-2}}B_{1}^{\frac{r-2}{r-1}}\left(B_{1}+B_{2}\right)^{\frac{r}{r-1}}.\end{split}

In the above, the first inequality follows by an appropriate application of Lemma
4.3
(with
q=2
and by recalling that
\mathfrak{m}^{-1}\in\mathcal{W}_{\mu^{-1/2}}
,
c\leq c_{\mathfrak{m}^{-1}}
). The second inequality is a result of
L^{p}
-interpolation. The second-to-last inequality is due to Hölder’s inequality with the exponent
\frac{1}{2\Lambda}=\frac{r-1}{r-2}
and its conjugate
\frac{1}{1-2\Lambda}=r-1
. The final inequality is due to (
4.18
). Using that
z\mapsto z^{\frac{r}{r-1}}
is convex, we can estimate further as

\displaystyle\|\varphi_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{2}\|b_{0}{\bf m}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim TC_{T}^{\frac{2r(1-\theta)}{r-2}}(B_{1}^{2}+B_{1}^{\frac{r-2}{r-1}}B_{2}^{\frac{r}{r-1}})

\displaystyle\lesssim TC_{T}^{\frac{2r(1-\theta)}{r-2}}(B_{1}^{2}+B_{2}^{2}),

the last line following by Young’s inequality with exponent
\frac{1}{\Lambda}=\frac{2(r-1)}{r-2}
and its conjugate. Now, for all
c\leq\min\{c_{1},c_{\mathfrak{m}^{-1}},c_{\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{-1}}\}
and
M,R
sufficiently large, Lemma
4.3
shows that

\displaystyle\|b_{t}\|_{L^{q}_{\mathfrak{m}_{1}}(\mathcal{O})}\leq e^{-\lambda^{\prime}(T-t)}\|b_{T}\|_{L^{q}_{\mathfrak{m}_{1}}(\mathcal{O})}\quad\forall t\in[0,T],\quad\forall q\in[1,2],

which shows

(4.20)

\begin{split}&TC_{T}^{\frac{2r(1-\theta)}{r-2}}(B_{1}^{2}+B_{2}^{2})\\
&\lesssim TC_{T}^{\frac{2r(1-\theta)}{r-2}}\left(\left(\int_{0}^{T}(\varphi^{\prime}/\varphi)^{1/2\Lambda}\|b_{t}\mathfrak{m}_{1}\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}\right)^{2}+\left(\int_{0}^{T}(\varphi^{\prime}/\varphi)^{1/\Lambda}\|b_{t}\mathfrak{m}_{1}\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}\right)^{2}\right)\\
&\lesssim TC_{T}^{\frac{2r(1-\theta)}{r-2}}\|b_{T}\mathfrak{m}_{1}\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(\int_{0}^{T}(\varphi^{\prime}/\varphi)^{1/(i\Lambda)}\right)^{2}.\end{split}

Inserting (
4.20
) into (
4.19
) and then changing variables back to
\varphi_{0}
, we have established

\displaystyle\|b_{0}{\bf m}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{O})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\varphi_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{-1}T^{\frac{1}{2}}C_{T}^{\frac{r(1-\theta)}{r-2}}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{2}T^{1-\frac{1}{i\Lambda}}\left(\int_{0}^{1}(\varphi_{0}^{\prime}/\varphi_{0})^{1/(i\Lambda)}\right)^{2}}\|b_{T}\mathfrak{m}_{1}\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{-\vartheta}\|\varphi_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}^{-1}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{2}\left(\int_{0}^{1}(\varphi_{0}^{\prime}/\varphi_{0})^{1/(i\Lambda)}\right)^{2}}\|b_{T}\mathfrak{m}_{1}\|_{L^{1}(\mathcal{O})}\quad\forall T\in(0,1],

where

\vartheta=\frac{r+5}{2(r-2)}-\frac{r(1-\theta)}{r-2}\left(\frac{(2d+1)(p+2)}{2p}-2d-\frac{1}{2}\right)>0.

We conclude by choosing any
0\leq\varphi_{0}\in\mathcal{D}((0,1))
appropriately so that

\|\varphi_{0}^{\prime}\|_{L^{2}(0,1)}>0,\quad\int_{0}^{1}(\varphi_{0}^{\prime}/\varphi_{0})^{1/(i\Lambda)}<\infty,\quad\forall i\in\{1,2\}.

∎

Restating everything in terms of the forward equation, we now have the
L^{2}\to L^{\infty}
ultracontractivity property of the solution to (
4.7
) as follows.

Proposition 4.10
.

We adopt the assumptions and notations of Lemma
4.9
, so that in particular
c\leq\min\{c_{1},c_{\mathfrak{m}^{-1}},c_{\mathfrak{m}_{1}^{-1}}\}
. Denote
{\bf w}:=\mathfrak{m}^{-1}
. Assume that
\omega_{\star}\in\mathcal{W}_{\mu^{-1/2}}
is another weight satisfying

\left\|\frac{\omega_{\star}}{{\bf w}}\langle v\rangle^{2-\theta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=:C_{\omega_{\star},{\bf w}}<\infty.

Then for every
0\leq s<t
,

(4.21)

\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,s)h_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}}(\mathcal{O})}\lesssim C_{\omega_{\star},{\bf w}}\frac{e^{-\lambda^{\prime}(t-s)}}{\min\{(t-s)^{\vartheta},1\}}\|h_{s}\|_{L^{2}_{{\bf w}}(\mathcal{O})}.

Proof
. All of the results can be obtained for general
0\leq s<t
by time translation, so we will only consider the case
s=0
and
t=T
.

First, we discuss the case
t-s\leq 1
,
i.e.

T\leq 1
. Choosing any
\widetilde{\omega}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
with
\widetilde{\omega}\omega_{\star}^{-1}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and using the fact that that
L^{2}_{\widetilde{\omega}^{-1}}
is dense in
L^{1}_{\omega_{\star}^{-1}}(\mathcal{O})
, we can compute the
L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}}
-norm of
h_{T}
as follows

(4.22)

\begin{split}\|h_{T}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}}}&=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{T}\in L^{2}_{\widetilde{\omega}}(\mathcal{O}),\\
\|b_{T}\|_{L^{1}_{\omega_{\star}^{-1}}}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\iint_{\mathcal{O}}h_{T}b_{T}\\
&=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{T}\in L^{2}_{\widetilde{\omega}}(\mathcal{O}),\\
\|b_{T}\|_{L^{1}_{\omega_{\star}^{-1}}}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\iint_{\mathcal{O}}h_{0}b_{0}\\
&\leq\|h_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{{\bf w}}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{T}\in L^{2}_{\widetilde{\omega}}(\mathcal{O}),\\
\|b_{T}\|_{L^{1}_{\omega_{\star}^{-1}}}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\|b_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{m}}}\\
&\lesssim\frac{1}{\min\{T^{\vartheta},1\}}\|h_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{{\bf w}}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}b_{T}\in L^{2}_{\widetilde{\omega}}(\mathcal{O}),\\
\|b_{T}\|_{L^{1}_{\omega_{\star}^{-1}}}\leq 1\end{subarray}}\|b_{T}\|_{L^{1}_{\mathfrak{m}_{1}}(\mathcal{O})}.\end{split}

In the above, the second line is due to the duality formula in Lemma
2.3
. The last line follows by applying Lemma
4.9
. From the conclusion of (
4.22
), we deduce (
4.21
) for every
T\in(0,1]
, because in this range we have
1\lesssim e^{-\lambda^{\prime}T}
.

For the general case of
T>1
, we write

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(T,0)h_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}}}

\displaystyle=\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(T,T-1)\,\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(T-1,0)\,h_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(T,T-1)\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{{\bf w}},L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}})}\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(T-1,0)\,h_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{{\bf w}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{\omega_{\star},{\bf w}}e^{-\lambda^{\prime}}\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(T-1,0)\,h_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{{\bf w}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\omega_{\star},{\bf w}}e^{-\lambda^{\prime}T}\|h_{0}\|_{L^{2}_{{\bf w}}},

where we used (
4.21
) for the case
t-s\leq 1
to estimate
\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(T,T-1)\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{{\bf w}},L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}})}
;
the last inequality is due to Proposition
4.4
with
p=2
(recall that we assume
c\leq c_{\mathfrak{m}^{-1}}
). This completes the proof.
∎

4.5.
Well-posedness of the full operator
\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]
in a reference space

In this section and those to follow, we return to the discussion of the full operator
\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]=\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]+\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]
. Here, let us now mention the well-posedness of (
4.4
), namely the evolution equation associated to
\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]
, which we recall here:

(4.23)

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}h=\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]h,\\
h|_{t=0}=h_{0},\\
\gamma_{-}h=\gamma_{+}h\circ\mathscr{V}_{x}.\end{cases}

We also remind the reader that
\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]=-v\cdot\nabla_{x}+\mathsf{L}_{\varsigma}[k]+\mathsf{P}_{\varsigma}[k]
, where

\displaystyle\mathsf{L}_{\varsigma}[k]h

\displaystyle:=\sigma_{\varsigma}[k]\Delta_{v}h+\zeta[k]\cdot\nabla_{v}h+\eta[k]h,

\displaystyle\sigma_{\varsigma}[k]

\displaystyle:=1+\frac{1}{d}E[k]-\frac{1}{d}{\bf A}_{\varsigma}[k],\quad{\bf A}_{\varsigma}[k]:=\frac{|j[k]|*_{t}{\bf s}_{\varsigma}\star_{\varsigma}{\bf r}_{\varsigma}|^{2}}{1+\rho[k]*_{t}{\bf s}_{\varsigma}\star_{\varsigma}{\bf r}_{\varsigma}},

\displaystyle\zeta[k]

\displaystyle:=v(1+\rho[k])-j[k],\quad\eta[k]:=d(1+\rho[k]),

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}_{\varsigma}[k]h

\displaystyle:=\Big((1+\rho[k])j[h]-j[k]\rho[h]\Big)\cdot v\mu

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{d}\left(-\Big(d+E[k]-{\bf A}_{\varsigma}[k]\Big)\rho[h]-j[k]\cdot j[h]+(1+\rho[k])E[h]\right)(|v|^{2}-d)\mu.

Proposition 4.11
.

Let
\omega_{1}\in\mathcal{W}
. We assume that
k\in\mathcal{X}_{\omega,c}
where
c\leq\min\{c_{1},c_{\omega_{1}}\}
(see Lemmas
4.1
–
4.2
). Then for each
h_{0}\in L^{2}_{\omega_{1}}(\mathcal{O})
, the system (
4.23
) is well-posed in
C([0,T];L^{2}_{\omega_{1}}(\mathcal{O}))
. Consequently we may associate to (
4.23
) an evolution system
(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,s))
for which
\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,s)h_{s}=h_{t}
for every
0\leq s\leq t
and
h_{s}\in L^{2}_{\omega_{1}}(\mathcal{O})
. Furthermore, the associated solution
h
satisfies the conservation laws in the following sense: denoting

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}:=\{h_{0}\in L^{1}(\mathcal{O},\langle v\rangle^{2}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v)\;|\;\langle\!\langle h_{0}\rangle\!\rangle=\langle\!\langle|v|^{2}h_{0}\rangle\!\rangle=\langle\!\langle(R(x)\cdot v)h_{0}\rangle\!\rangle=0\quad\forall R\in\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}\},

\displaystyle\langle\!\langle h_{0}\rangle\!\rangle:=\iint_{\mathcal{O}}h_{0}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v,

and writing
\Pi:L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}(\mathcal{O})\to\mathcal{C}
as the orthogonal projection operator, we have

(I-\Pi)\,\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,s)h_{s}=\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,s)(I-\Pi)h_{s}\quad\forall h_{s}\in L^{2}_{\widetilde{\omega}}(\mathcal{O}).

Consequently, provided that
h_{0}\in\mathcal{C}
, we have
h_{t}\in\mathcal{C}
for every
t\geq 0
.

Proof
. The well-posedness just follows by checking the assumptions of Proposition
2.1
. Under the assumption of
k\in\mathcal{X}_{\omega,c}
and
c\leq\min\{c_{1},c_{\omega_{1}}\}
, the conditions (L1)–(L2)–(L3) are easy to check, and in particular (L2) follows by the choice of
c_{\omega_{1}}
(see Lemma
4.2
). We will therefore only verify (P1). Namely, we need to prove that

(4.24)

\iint_{\mathcal{O}}|\mathsf{P}_{\varsigma}[k]|^{2}\omega_{1}^{2}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v\lesssim\iint_{\mathcal{O}}|f|^{2}\omega_{1}^{2}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v.

Since
k\in\mathcal{X}_{\omega,c}
, we observe that every coefficient in the definition of
\mathsf{P}_{\varsigma}[k]h
may be bounded as

\displaystyle\Big|(1+\rho[k])j[h]-j[k]\rho[h]\Big|\lesssim_{c}|j[h]|+|\rho[h]|,

\displaystyle\left|-(d+E[k]-{\bf A}_{\varsigma}[k])\rho[h]-j[k]\cdot j[h]+(1+\rho[k])E[h]\right|\lesssim_{c}|\rho[h]|+|j[h]|+|E[h]|.

Henceforth,

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{O}}|\mathsf{P}_{\varsigma}[k]|^{2}\omega_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\iint_{\mathcal{O}}(|\rho[h]|+|j[h]|+|E[h]|)^{2}\langle v\rangle^{4}\mu^{2}\omega_{1}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\langle v\rangle^{4}\mu^{2}\omega_{1}^{2}\right)\left(\int_{\Omega}(|\rho[h]|+|j[h]|+|E[h]|)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\Omega}(|\rho[h]|+|j[h]|+|E[h]|)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\Omega}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\langle v\rangle^{2}h\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{\Omega}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}h^{2}\omega_{1}^{2}\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\langle v\rangle^{4}\omega_{1}^{-2}\right)

\displaystyle=C\iint_{\mathcal{O}}h^{2}\omega_{1}^{2}.

In the above, we took advantage of the fact that
\langle v\rangle^{2}\mu\omega_{1},\langle v\rangle^{2}\omega_{1}^{-1}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. This proves (
4.24
) and thus (P1).

Next, we verify that the conservation laws are satisfied by
h
,
i.e.

h\in\mathcal{C}
. It is enough to observe that since
L^{\infty}_{\omega_{1}}\hookrightarrow L^{1}_{\langle v\rangle^{2}}
, any
\chi\in\mathcal{D}(\overline{\mathcal{O}})
satisfying
\chi\lesssim\langle v\rangle^{2}
is admissible as a test function in the Green’s formula for
h
. By construction (see (
4.2
)–(
4.3
)), we have
\iint_{\mathcal{O}}(1,v,|v|^{2})(\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}+{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k])h=0
. Recalling that
\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]=-v\cdot\nabla_{x}+\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}+{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]
, the conservation laws follow immediately.
∎

4.6.
Hypocoercivity of the full operator
\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]
in a reference space

In this section we establish the hypocoercivity property of the operator
\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]
in the most typical
L^{2}
-space which will serve as a reference point later. Let us remind the reader that

\mathcal{C}:=\{h_{0}\in L^{1}(\mathcal{O},\langle v\rangle^{2}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v)\;|\;\langle\!\langle h_{0}\rangle\!\rangle=\langle\!\langle|v|^{2}h_{0}\rangle\!\rangle=\langle\!\langle(R(x)\cdot v)h_{0}\rangle\!\rangle=0\quad\forall R\in\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}\}.

Proposition 4.12
.

There exists
c_{2}>0
small enough such that the following assertion is true: if
k\in\mathcal{X}_{\omega,c}
with
c\leq\min\{c_{1},c_{2}\}
, then there exists a scalar product
(\!(\cdot,\cdot)\!)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}
on
L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}(\mathcal{O})
such that the induced norm
{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\cdot\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}
is equivalent to
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}
, and moreover the Dirichlet form corresponding to
\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]
satisfies

(\!(-\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]f,f)\!)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}\gtrsim\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}

for every
f\in\textnormal{Dom}(\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k])\cap\mathcal{C}
satisfying the specular reflection boundary condition (
1.2
).

Toward the proof of this proposition we introduce some handy notations, following the work of
[
4
]
. After appropriately translating as necessary, we assume without loss of generality that

\int_{\Omega}x\,\textnormal{d}x=0,

so that by the arguments in
[
4
, Section 1.2]
we see that
\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}
now reduces to the
centered
infinitesimal rigid displacement fields:

\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}=\left\{U\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}\;\middle|\;\textnormal{$U$ is skew-symmetric and $Ux\cdot n_{x}=0$ $\forall x\in\partial\Omega$}\right\}.

Then, for a given source term
S\in L^{2}(\Omega)
, we denote by
u[S]\in H^{1}(\Omega)
the solution to the Poisson equation

(4.25)

\begin{cases}-\Delta_{x}u[S]=S&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\nabla_{x}u[S]\cdot n_{x}=0&\text{on }\partial\Omega.\end{cases}

We remark that whenever the compatibility condition
\int_{\Omega}S=0
holds, the classical Lax–Milgram theorem ensures the existence of
u[S]\in H^{1}
which solves (
4.25
) in the variational sense and satisfies
\int_{\Omega}u[S]=0
. An immediate consequence of the Lax–Milgram theorem is that

(4.26)

\|u[S]\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|S\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)},

but it is well-known that the regularity can be improved:

(4.27)

\|u[S]\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|S\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

In similar nature, for
S\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
we denote by
\widetilde{u}[S]\in H^{1}(\Omega)
the solution to the elliptic equation

(4.28)

\begin{cases}-\nabla_{x}\cdot(\nabla_{x}^{s}\widetilde{u}[S])=S&\text{in }\Omega,\\
\widetilde{u}[S]\cdot n_{x}=0&\text{on }\partial\Omega,\\
\nabla_{x}^{s}\widetilde{u}[S]n_{x}-(\nabla_{x}^{s}\widetilde{u}[S]:n_{x}\otimes n_{x})n_{x}=0&\text{on }\partial\Omega.\end{cases}

where
\nabla_{x}^{s}
stands for the symmetric gradient
(\nabla_{x}^{s}U)_{ij}:=\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{x_{j}}U_{i}+\partial_{x_{i}}U_{j})
. Now writing the skew-symmetric gradient as
(\nabla_{x}^{a})_{ij}:=\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{x_{j}}U_{i}-\partial_{x_{i}}U_{j})
, we define the Hilbert space

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}:=\left\{W\in H^{1}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})\;\middle|\;W\cdot n=0\text{ on }\partial\Omega\text{ and }\Pi_{\Omega}\int_{\Omega}\nabla^{a}W\,\textnormal{d}x=0\right\},

equipped with the
H^{1}(\Omega)
-norm, where
\Pi_{\Omega}
denotes the orthogonal projection onto
\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}
. From the results of
[
4
]
, for each
S\in L^{2}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{d})
we know the existence and uniqueness of
\widetilde{u}[S]\in\mathcal{V}
solving (
4.28
) in the variational sense, with (again thanks to the Lax–Milgram theorem)

(4.29)

\|\widetilde{u}[S]\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|S\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

If additionally
S
is such that
\int_{\Omega}S\cdot Ux=0
for every
U\in\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}
, then the following regularity estimate holds (see
[
4
, Theorem 2.11]
)

(4.30)

\|\widetilde{u}[S]\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|S\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

We then define the scalar product
(\!(\cdot,\cdot)\!)
as

\begin{split}(\!(f,h)\!)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}:&=(f,h)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}\\
&\quad+\delta_{1}(-\nabla_{x}u[\rho[f]],j[h])_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\delta_{1}(-\nabla_{x}u[\rho[h]],j[f])_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\quad+\delta_{2}(-\nabla_{x}^{s}\widetilde{u}[j[f]],\widetilde{M}[h])_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\delta_{2}(-\nabla_{x}^{s}\widetilde{u}[j[h]],\widetilde{M}[f])_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\quad+\delta_{3}(-\nabla_{x}u[\tau[f]],M[h])_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\delta_{3}(-\nabla_{x}u[\tau[h]],M[f])_{L^{2}(\Omega)},\end{split}

where
\delta_{i}>0
are constants to be chosen later, and

\begin{split}&\tau[f](x,v):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\frac{|v|^{2}-d}{\sqrt{2d}}f(x,v)\,\textnormal{d}v,\\
&M[f]:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}v(|v|^{2}-d-2)f\,\textnormal{d}v,\\
&\widetilde{M}[f]:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(v\otimes v-I_{d\times d})f\,\textnormal{d}v.\end{split}

In the above,
I_{d\times d}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
is the identity matrix, and
(\cdot,\cdot)_{L^{2}}
refers to the usual
L^{2}
scalar product. Here we point out that if
f
satisfies the specular reflection boundary condition (
1.2
), then there is no flux of mass through the boundary,
i.e.

\forall x\in\partial\Omega,\quad\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\gamma f\,(n_{x}\cdot v)\,\textnormal{d}v=0,

and therefore
j[f]\cdot n_{x}=0
whenever
x\in\partial\Omega
. Furthermore, if
f\in\mathcal{C}
then by its definition we have
\iint_{\mathcal{O}}Ux\cdot vf\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v=0
for every
U\in\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}
, which translates to
\int_{\Omega}Ux\cdot j[f]\,\textnormal{d}x=0
for every
U\in\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}
. This ensures that the regularity estimate (
4.30
) applies to
\widetilde{u}[j[f]]
. In similar nature, if
f\in\mathcal{C}
then
\langle\!\langle f\rangle\!\rangle=\int_{\Omega}\rho[f]=0
and so
u[\rho[f]]
obeys the estimate in (
4.27
).

Prior to making computations on
(\!(\cdot,\cdot)\!)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}
it is worth mentioning that the “linearized operator part” of
\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]
, namely
-v\cdot\nabla_{x}+\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}
, is quite well-behaved and falls into the scope of
[
4
]
. In particular, the main result of
[
4
]
immediately guarantees that there exist suitable choices of
\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3}>0
such that the scalar product
(\!(\cdot,\cdot)\!)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}
satisfies

(4.31)

(\!((-v\cdot\nabla_{x}+\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin})f,f)\!)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}\lesssim-\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}

for every
f
which satisfies the specular reflection boundary condition. Unfortunately, the result (
4.31
) as it stands is not enough to incorporate the terms which arise from the “nonlinear part”
\Upsilon^{\perp}_{k,\varsigma}
. Indeed, we cannot expect the right-hand side of (
4.31
) to be able to absorb all of the terms which arise from it, especially due to the fact that
\Upsilon^{\perp}_{k,\varsigma}
is part of the principal operator of
\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]
. Thus, it is necessary for us to go back to the level of basic computations regarding
\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}
where derivatives are still present.

Now, we establish some properties of the linear operators in discussion, which will aid in the forthcoming analysis. Let us denote by
\mathsf{C}_{\rm FP}
the linear Fokker–Planck operator:

\begin{split}\mathsf{C}_{\rm FP}f&:=\Delta_{v}f+\nabla_{v}\cdot(vf),\\
\end{split}

so that
\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}
is nothing but

\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}f=\mathsf{C}_{\rm FP}f+\sum_{i=1}^{d}\pi_{i}f+2\pi_{d+1}f.

When the transport operator is also taken into effect, we will denote

\mathsf{C}_{\rm full}f:=-v\cdot\nabla_{x}f+\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}f.

Lemma 4.13
.

The following properties hold.

(1)

The operator
\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}
is self-adjoint and its kernel in
L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{v})
is given by

K:=\textnormal{Span}\{\mu,v_{1}\mu,\ldots,v_{d}\mu,|v|^{2}\mu\},

so that
\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}\pi=0
.

(2)

For any
f\in\textnormal{Dom}(\mathsf{C}_{\rm full})
satisfying the specular reflection boundary condition (
1.2
),

(\mathsf{C}_{\rm full}f,f)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}=(\mathsf{C}_{\rm FP}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f,{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}},

where
(\cdot,\cdot)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}
is the scalar product defined on
L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}(\mathcal{O})
:

(f,h)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}:=\iint_{\mathcal{O}}fh\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v,

and
{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f:=(I-\pi)f
.

Proof
. A straightforward computation shows that
K\subset\textnormal{Ker}(\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin})
. The opposite inclusion also follows easily. Indeed, let
f\in\textnormal{Dom}(\mathsf{C}_{\rm FP})\subset L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{v})
and observe that

\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}f=0\implies\mathsf{C}_{\rm FP}f\in K,

because
\pi_{i}f
are trivially members of
K
. We can then explicitly solve this differential inclusion to prove that
f\in K
.

To see the second assertion, we compute

\displaystyle(\mathsf{C}_{\rm full}f,f)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}f,f\right)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f,\pi f+{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\right)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f,{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{C}_{\rm FP}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f,{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}.

The right-hand side of the first line follows from the fact that the transport term vanishes, owing to the Gauss–Ostrogradsky theorem and the specular reflection boundary condition. To obtain the second line, we used the fact that
\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}\pi f=0
. The third line is due to the fact that
\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}
is self-adjoint and again that
\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}\pi=0
. Finally the last line comes from
\pi_{i}(\mathsf{I}-\pi)=0
for all
i=0,1,\ldots,d+1
.
∎

A direct consequence of Lemma
4.13
is the following. Although the proof follows more or less classical estimates regarding the linear Fokker–Planck operator, we fully include the arguments for the sake of completeness.

Lemma 4.14
.

For any
f\in\textnormal{Dom}(\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k])\cap\mathcal{C}
satisfying (
1.2
), we have the coercivity estimate

\begin{split}(-\mathsf{C}_{\rm full}f,f)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}\gtrsim\|\nabla_{v}(\mu^{-1}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f)\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{+1/2}}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{v}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}+\|v\,{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}+\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}.\end{split}

Proof
. Thanks to Lemma
4.13
, we have the explicit identity

(4.32)

(-\mathsf{C}_{\rm full}f,f)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}=(-\mathsf{C}_{\rm FP}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f,{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}=\iint_{\mathcal{O}}|\nabla_{v}(\mu^{-1}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f)|^{2}\,\mu\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v.

Expanding the square and the gradient, we observe that the integral of the right-hand side is equal to

(4.33)

\begin{split}\iint_{\mathcal{O}}\left|\nabla_{v}(\mu^{-1}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f)\right|^{2}\mu\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v&=\iint_{\mathcal{O}}\left|v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f+\nabla_{v}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\right|^{2}\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v\\
&=\iint_{\mathcal{O}}|v|^{2}|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f|^{2}\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v+\int|\nabla_{v}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f|^{2}\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v\\
&\quad+2\iint_{\mathcal{O}}(v\mu^{-1}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f)\cdot(\mu^{-1}\nabla_{v}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f)\mu\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v\\
&=\iint_{\mathcal{O}}\Big(-d|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f|^{2}+|\nabla_{v}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f|^{2}\Big)\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v,\end{split}

where the integral
\iint_{\mathcal{O}}|v|^{2}|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f|^{2}\mu^{-1}
vanished because the crossing term satisfies:

(4.34)

\begin{split}2\iint_{\mathcal{O}}(v\mu^{-1}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f)\cdot(\mu^{-1}\nabla_{v}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f)\mu\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v&=2\iint_{\mathcal{O}}v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\cdot\nabla_{v}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\,\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v\\
&=\iint_{\mathcal{O}}v\cdot\nabla_{v}|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f|^{2}\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v\\
&=-\iint_{\mathcal{O}}|v|^{2}|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f|^{2}\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}v-d\iint_{\mathcal{O}}|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f|^{2}\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}v.\end{split}

Hence, from (
4.32
) and (
4.33
) we currently have

(4.35)

(-\mathsf{C}_{\rm full}f,f)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}\gtrsim\|\nabla_{v}(\mu^{-1}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f)\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mu^{+1/2}}}+\|\nabla_{v}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}-\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}.

That last negative term does not create any problem, because the Poincaré inequality applied to the Gaussian measure gives

(4.36)

(-\mathsf{C}_{\rm full}f,f)\gtrsim\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}.

It thus remains for us to somehow restore the term
\|v\,{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}
to the right-hand side. Let us take advantage of the presence of
\|\nabla_{v}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}
in (
4.35
). In order to obtain an identity for
\|\nabla_{v}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}
which is not as damaging as in (
4.34
), we introduce a slightly variational term as follows: for any
\alpha,\beta>0

\begin{split}\iint_{\mathcal{O}}\left|\alpha v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f+\beta\nabla_{v}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\right|^{2}\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v&=\alpha^{2}\|v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}+\beta^{2}\|\nabla_{v}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}\\
&\quad+2\alpha\beta\int v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\cdot\nabla_{v}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\,\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v\\
&=\beta^{2}\|\nabla_{v}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}+\alpha\left(\alpha-\beta\right)\|v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}-d\alpha\beta\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}.\end{split}

Equivalently we have

\displaystyle\beta^{2}\|\nabla_{v}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\alpha v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f+\beta\nabla_{v}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\right\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}-\alpha\left(\alpha-\beta\right)\|v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}+d\alpha\beta\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}.

Choosing
\beta=1
and
\alpha=1/2
, we get

\|\nabla_{v}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}=\left\|\alpha v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f+\beta\nabla_{v}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\right\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\|v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}+\frac{d}{2}\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2},

so in particular

\|\nabla_{v}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}\gtrsim\|v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}.

Inserting this into (
4.35
) we deduce

(4.37)

\begin{split}(-\mathsf{C}_{\rm full}f,f)&\gtrsim\|v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}-\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}.\end{split}

Combining (
4.35
)–(
4.36
)–(
4.37
) we complete the proof.
∎

As is typical with kinetic collisional operators, at the moment we only have a coercivity estimate with respect to the orthogonal complement. We can proceed further by using the arguments from
[
4
]
, then combining them with Lemma
4.14
, in order to obtain full coercivity of the linearized operator
\mathsf{L}
with respect to the twisted product
(\!(\cdot,\cdot)\!)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}
. Note that below, we keep the derivative term, and also the weighted norm
\|v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}
, as aforementioned, they are both crucial in absorbing the terms which arise from
{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]
(see Lemmas
4.16
–
4.17
below).

Lemma 4.15
.

There exist suitable choices of
\delta_{1},\delta_{2},\delta_{3}>0
such that for any
f\in\textnormal{Dom}(\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k])\cap\mathcal{C}
satisfying (
1.2
),

(\!(-\mathsf{C}_{\rm full}f,f)\!)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}\gtrsim\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{v}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1})\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{+1/2}}}^{2}+\|v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}.

Proof
. We only sketch the proof and refer to
[
4
]
for the details. Thanks to Lemma
4.14
and the results of
[
4
, Section 3]
(namely (
4.27
)–(
4.30
)), there exist
C_{i}>0
and
C>0
such that

\begin{split}&(\!(-\mathsf{C}_{\rm full}f,f)\!)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}\\
&\geq C_{0}\left(\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{v}(\mu^{-1}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f)\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{+1/2}}}^{2}+\|v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}\right)\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt+\delta_{1}\left(C_{1}\|\rho[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}-C\Big(\|j[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\tau[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}\Big)\right)\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt+\delta_{2}\left(C_{2}\|j[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}-C\Big(\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}\|\rho\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}-\|\tau[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\rho\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}-\|\tau[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}-\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}\Big)\right)\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt+\delta_{3}\left(C_{3}\|\tau[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}-C\Big(\|j[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}-\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}\Big)\right).\end{split}

Here Lemma
4.14
was used to obtain the first line. Proceeding as in
[
4
, Proof of Theorem 1.1]
, we can then choose
0\ll\delta_{1}\ll\delta_{2}\ll\delta_{3}\ll 1
appropriately small enough so that

\begin{split}(\!(-\mathsf{C}_{\rm full}f,f)\!)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}&\gtrsim\Big(\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}+\|\rho[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|j[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\tau[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\Big)\\
&\quad+\|\nabla_{v}(\mu^{-1}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f)\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{+1/2}}}^{2}+\|v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}.\end{split}

The right-hand side of the first line is equivalent to
\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}
, and we therefore obtain the desired result.
∎

In order to extend to result to
(\!(-\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]f,f)\!)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}
, it is now enough to prove that the nonlinear remainder does not alter the scalar product too much. We recall that the nonlinear remainder satisfies (by definition)

\rho[{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]f]=j[{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]f]=\tau[{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]f]=0,

which allows us to write

\begin{split}(\!(\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]f,f)\!)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}&=(\!(\mathsf{C}_{\rm full}f,f)\!)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}+({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]f,f)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2})}}\\
&\quad+\delta_{2}(-\nabla_{x}^{s}\widetilde{u}[j[f]],\widetilde{M}[{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]f])_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\quad+\delta_{3}(-\nabla_{x}u[\tau[f]],M[{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]f])_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.\end{split}

We proceed to estimate the remaining terms on the right-hand side, one by one.

Lemma 4.16
.

We assume that
\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\ll 1
. Then for any
f\in\textnormal{Dom}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k])
, the following estimate holds:

\begin{split}&\left|({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]f,f)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}\right|\\
&\leq C\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\left(\|\nabla_{v}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1})\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{+1/2}}}^{2}+\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}+\|v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}\right).\end{split}

Proof
. We first perform the computation

\displaystyle({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]f,f)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}

\displaystyle=({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]f,{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f)_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}

(4.38)

\displaystyle=\iint_{\mathcal{O}}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\left(\rho[k]\mathsf{C}_{\rm FP}f+\frac{1}{d}\left(E[k]-{\bf A}_{\varsigma}[k]\right)\Delta_{v}f-j[k]\cdot\nabla_{v}f\right)\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v,

where the second line is due to the fact that
\pi{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]=0
; the last equality then follows since
\pi{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f=0
. We now estimate each term in the last line (
4.38
).

To compute and estimate the first term in (
4.38
), we decompose
f={}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f+\rho[f]\mu+j[f]\cdot v\mu+\tau[f]\frac{|v|^{2}-d}{\sqrt{2d}}\mu
, then use the eigenvalue identities

\mathsf{C}_{\rm FP}(\mu,v_{i}\mu,(|v|^{2}-d)\mu)=(0,-v_{i}\mu,-2(|v|^{2}-d)\mu)\\

in order to write

(4.39)

\begin{split}&\iint_{\mathcal{O}}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\rho[k]\mathsf{C}_{\rm FP}f\,\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v\\
&=\iint_{\mathcal{O}}\rho[k]\left({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mathsf{C}_{\rm FP}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\,\mu^{-1}-{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>fj[f]\cdot v-2{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\,\tau[f]\frac{|v|^{2}-d}{\sqrt{2d}}\right)\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v.\end{split}

On one hand, by standard computations on the Fokker–Planck operator, we can immediately compute

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{O}}\rho[k]{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mathsf{C}_{\rm FP}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\,\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v

\displaystyle=-\iint_{\mathcal{O}}\rho[k]|\nabla_{v}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1})|^{2}\mu\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v

\displaystyle\lesssim\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\|\nabla_{v}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1})\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{+1/2}}}^{2}.

On the other hand, the remaining terms on the right-hand side of (
4.39
) vanish thanks to the identities

(4.40)

j[{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f]=\tau[{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f]=0.

Therefore, the first term in the right-hand side of (
4.38
) is simply bounded as

(4.41)

\left|\iint_{\mathcal{O}}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\rho[k]\mathsf{C}_{\rm FP}f\,\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v\right|\lesssim\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\|\nabla_{v}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1})\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{+1/2}}}^{2}.

Next, we move on to compute the term in (
4.38
) containing
\Delta_{v}f
. We observe that

(4.42)

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\Delta_{v}f\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}v\\
&=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{v}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1})\cdot\nabla_{v}f\,\textnormal{d}v\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{v}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1})\cdot(\nabla_{v}(f\mu^{-1})-vf\mu^{-1})\mu\,\textnormal{d}v\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{v}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1})\cdot\left(\nabla_{v}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1})+j[f]+\sqrt{\frac{2}{d}}\tau[f]v\right)\mu\,\textnormal{d}v-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{v}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1})\cdot(vf)\,\textnormal{d}v\\
&=\|\nabla_{v}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1})\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{+1/2}}}^{2}-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1})\cdot\left(j[f]v\mu+\sqrt{\frac{2}{d}}\tau[f](d-|v|^{2})\mu\right)\\
&\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1})\nabla_{v}\cdot(vf)\,\textnormal{d}v\\
&=\|\nabla_{v}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1})\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{+1/2}}}^{2}+j[f]\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\,\textnormal{d}v+\sqrt{\frac{2}{d}}\tau[f]\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(|v|^{2}-d){}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\,\textnormal{d}v\\
&\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1}(df+v\cdot\nabla_{v}f)\,\textnormal{d}v\\
&=\|\nabla_{v}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1})\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{+1/2}}}^{2}+d\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}\\
&\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1}\left(v\cdot\nabla_{v}\left({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f+\rho[f]\mu+j[f]\cdot v\mu+\tau[f]\frac{|v|^{2}-d}{\sqrt{2d}}\mu\right)\right)\,\textnormal{d}v,\end{split}

the last line following again from (
4.40
). It remains to estimate the integral in the last line. A direct computation first gives

(4.43)

\begin{split}\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1}v\cdot\nabla_{v}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\,\textnormal{d}v\right|&=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla_{v}\cdot(v\mu^{-1})|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f|^{2}\,\textnormal{d}v\right|\\
&\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(|v|^{2}+d)|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f|^{2}\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}v\\
&\lesssim\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{v})}^{2}+\|v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{v})}^{2},\end{split}

and for the remaining terms containing the macroscopic variables, we can estimate in the following way. For instance:

(4.44)

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1}v\cdot\nabla_{v}\left(\tau[f]\frac{|v|^{2}-d}{\sqrt{2d}}\mu\right)\,\textnormal{d}v\\
&=\tau[f]\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f|v|^{2}\left(\sqrt{\frac{2}{d}}-\frac{|v|^{2}-d}{\sqrt{2d}}\right)\,\textnormal{d}v\\
&\leq|\tau[f]|\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{v})}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}|v|^{4}\left(\sqrt{\frac{2}{d}}-\frac{|v|^{2}-d}{\sqrt{2d}}\right)^{2}\mu\,\textnormal{d}v\right)^{1/2}\\
&\lesssim|\tau[f]|^{2}+\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d}_{v})},\end{split}

where we used the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality. Therefore, by inserting (
4.43
)–(
4.44
) into (
4.42
), we deduce that the second term in (
4.38
) is bounded as

(4.45)

\begin{split}&\iint_{\mathcal{O}}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\left(E[k]-{\bf A}_{\varsigma}[k]\right)\Delta_{v}f\,\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v\\
&\lesssim\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}(1+\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}})\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\Bigg(\|\nabla_{v}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1})\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{+1/2}}}+\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}+\|v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}\\
&\hskip 170.71652pt+\|\rho[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|j[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\tau[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\Bigg).\end{split}

We now estimate the final term in (
4.38
). A direct computation shows

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathcal{O}}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>fj[k]\cdot\nabla_{v}f\,\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v

\displaystyle=\iint_{\mathcal{O}}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>fj[k]\cdot\nabla_{v}\left({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f+\rho[f]\mu+j[f]\cdot v\mu+\tau[f]\frac{|v|^{2}-d}{\sqrt{2d}}\mu\right)\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v

\displaystyle=\iint_{\mathcal{O}}\frac{1}{2}j[k]\cdot\nabla_{v}|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f|^{2}\,\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v

\displaystyle\quad+\iint_{\mathcal{O}}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>fj[k]\cdot\nabla_{v}\left(\rho[f]\mu+j[f]\cdot v\mu+\tau[f]\frac{|v|^{2}-d}{\sqrt{2d}}\mu\right)\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v

\displaystyle=:I_{1}+I_{2}.

The Gauss–Ostrogradsky theorem and Young’s inequality show

|I_{1}|\lesssim\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\iint_{\mathcal{O}}(1+|v|^{2})|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f|^{2}\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v\leq\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\left(\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}+\|vf\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}\right).

For the terms in
I_{2}
we estimate with almost the same method as in (
4.44
), to establish

|I_{2}|\lesssim\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\left(\|\rho[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|j[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\tau[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}\right).

In all, it follows that the last term in (
4.38
) is bounded as

(4.46)

\begin{split}&\left|\iint_{\mathcal{O}}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>fj[k]\cdot\nabla_{v}f\,\mu^{-1}\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v\right|\\
&\lesssim\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\left(\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}+\|vf\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}+\|\rho[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|j[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\tau[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}\right).\end{split}

Collecting (
4.41
)–(
4.45
)–(
4.46
), and keeping in mind
\|\rho[f],j[f],\tau[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}(\mathcal{O})}
, we obtain the estimate announced in the lemma.
∎

Lemma 4.17
.

For any
f\in\textnormal{Dom}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k])
, if
\|k\|_{L^{\infty}}\ll 1
, then the following estimate holds:

\begin{split}&(-\nabla_{x}^{s}\widetilde{u}[j[f]],\widetilde{M}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]f))_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2},\\
&(-\nabla_{x}u[\tau[f]],M[{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]f])_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}.\end{split}

Proof
. We consider any
\omega_{\bullet}:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that
\nabla_{v}\omega_{\bullet}
and
\Delta_{v}\omega_{\bullet}
are of polynomial growth. Then we compute

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\omega_{\bullet}(v){}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]f\,\textnormal{d}v

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\omega_{\bullet}(v)\left(\frac{1}{d}\left(E[k]-{\bf A}_{\varsigma}[k]\right)\Delta_{v}f-j[k]\cdot\nabla_{v}f+\rho[k]\nabla_{v}\cdot(vf)\right)\,\textnormal{d}v

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\omega_{\bullet}(v)(\rho[k]j[f]-\rho[f]j[k])\cdot v\mu\,\textnormal{d}v

\displaystyle\quad-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\omega_{\bullet}(v)\frac{1}{d}\left(\left(E[k]-{\bf A}_{\varsigma}[k]\right)\rho[f]-\rho[k]E[f]+j[k]\cdot j[f]\right)(|v|^{2}-d)\mu\,\textnormal{d}v

\displaystyle=:I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}.

For
I_{1}
, the Gauss–Ostrogradsky formula gives

\displaystyle|I_{1}|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}-\nabla_{v}\omega_{\bullet}(v)\cdot\left(\frac{1}{d}\left(E[k]-{\bf A}_{\varsigma}[k]\right)\nabla_{v}f-j[k]f+\rho[k]vf\right)\,\textnormal{d}v\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Delta_{v}\omega_{\bullet}(v)\frac{1}{d}\left(E[k]-{\bf A}_{\varsigma}[k]\right)f+\nabla_{v}\omega_{\bullet}(v)\cdot f(j[k]-v\rho[k])\,\textnormal{d}v\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\omega_{\bullet},d}\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}(1+\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}})\|f\|_{L^{2}_{v}(\mu^{-1/2})}.

For
I_{2}
, we simply have

\displaystyle|I_{2}|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\omega_{\bullet}(v)(\rho[k]j[f]-\rho[f]j[k])\cdot v\mu\,\textnormal{d}v\right|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\omega_{\bullet}}\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\Big(|j[f]|+|\rho[f]|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\omega_{\bullet}}\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}},

and the estimate for
I_{3}
is analogous. Combining these lead to

\|\widetilde{M}[{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|M[{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}.

We deduce, using (
4.26
)–(
4.29
), that

\displaystyle(-\nabla_{x}^{s}\widetilde{u}[j[f]],\widetilde{M}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]f))_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla_{x}^{s}\widetilde{u}[j[f]]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\widetilde{M}[{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|j[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2},

\displaystyle(-\nabla_{x}u[\tau[f]],M[{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]f])_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla_{x}u[\tau[f]]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|M[{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tau[f]\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}.

∎

4.6.1.
Proof of Proposition
4.12

Gathering Lemmas
4.15
–
4.16
–
4.17
, we obtain that

\displaystyle(\!(-\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]f,f)\!)

\displaystyle\gtrsim\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{v}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1})\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{+1/2}}}+\|v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}

\displaystyle\quad-C\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\Big(\|f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2}+\|\nabla_{v}({}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\mu^{-1})\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{+1/2}}}+\|v{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>f\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}\Big).

for every
\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\ll 1
and
f\in\textnormal{Dom}(\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k])\cap\mathcal{C}
satisfying the boundary condition (
1.2
). We just choose
c_{2}>0
so small that
c\leq c_{2}
and
k\in\mathcal{X}_{\omega,c}
imply the negative terms can be absorbed. This concludes the proof.

4.7.
Extension of the evolution system
\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}
to
L^{\infty}_{\omega}

Gathering the results of Sections
4.5
–
4.6
, we now establish the extension of the evolution system
\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}
to
L^{\infty}_{\omega}
and also its decay.

Proposition 4.18
.

There exists
c_{\star}>0
small enough so that if
c\leq c_{\star}
and
k\in\mathcal{X}_{\omega,c}
, then the evolution system as constructed in Proposition
4.11
satisfies the following decay estimate

\forall h_{s}\in L^{\infty}_{\omega}\cap\mathcal{C},\quad\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,s)h_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\lesssim e^{-\iota^{\prime}(t-s)}\|h_{s}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}},

up to constructive constants and some
\iota^{\prime}>0
which are independent of
\varsigma
.

The proof of Proposition
4.18
is quite long and so we split it into several lemmas which provide the appropriate necessary estimates.

4.7.1.
Proof of Proposition
4.18

(Step 1: Collecting previous estimates.) At this moment, we know from Proposition
4.11
that for
c\leq\min\{c_{1},c_{\mu^{-1/2}}\}
, the evolution equation corresponding to
\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]
is well-posed in
C([0,T];L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}(\mathcal{O}))
.

Consequently, combining with Proposition
4.12
, we have that for all
c\leq\min\{c_{1},c_{\mu^{-1/2}},c_{2}\}
, for every
\mathfrak{f}_{s}\in L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}\cap\mathcal{C}
there holds

(4.47)

\begin{split}\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,s)\mathfrak{f}_{s}\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}(\mathcal{O})}\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\lambda}(t-s)\|\mathfrak{f}_{s}\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}(\mathcal{O})},\end{split}

where for
\lambda>0
we denoted

\mathfrak{p}_{\lambda}(t):=e^{-\lambda t}.

On the other hand, in regard of the dissipative part of the operator
\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]
, we know from Proposition
4.4
that for each
\omega_{1}\in\mathcal{W}
, there exists
c_{\omega_{1}}>0
and
M_{\omega_{1}},R_{\omega_{1}}
such that
c\leq\min\{c_{1},c_{\omega_{1}}\}
,
M\geq M_{\omega_{1}}
, and
R\geq R_{\omega_{1}}
imply for some
\lambda^{\prime}>0

(4.48)

\forall p\in[2,\infty],\quad\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,s)\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{p}_{\omega_{1}})}\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\lambda^{\prime}}(t-s).

In similar nature, by applying
(
4.21
) of Proposition
4.10
with the specific choice of
{\bf w}=\mu^{-1/2}\approx e^{|v|^{2}/4}
, we know that for
c\leq\min\{c_{1},c_{\mu^{-1/2}},c_{\mu^{-1/2}\langle v\rangle^{\theta-2}}\}
, it holds for any weight
\omega_{\star}\in\mathcal{W}
that

(4.49)

\begin{split}&\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,s)\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}},L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}})}\lesssim\left\|\omega_{\star}\mu^{1/2}\langle v\rangle^{2-\theta}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\mathfrak{q}(t-s),\\
&\mathfrak{q}(t):=\frac{\mathfrak{p}_{\lambda^{\prime}}(t)}{\min\{t^{\vartheta},1\}}=e^{-\lambda^{\prime}t}\max\{t^{-\vartheta},1\},\end{split}

at least when
M,R
are sufficiently large (with respect to the choice of
\mu^{-1/2}
).

From hereon, we fix the exponential weight
\omega_{\star}
, say for instance
\omega_{\star}:=e^{|v|^{2}/8}
, so that the right-hand side of
\eqref{Eq/ Ultra}_{1}
is finite. In this way, we can fix
M,R>1
so large that, whenever
c\leq c_{\star}
and
k\in\mathcal{X}_{\omega,c}
, (i) (
4.48
) holds for all three choices of
\omega_{1}=\omega,\mu^{-1/2},\omega_{\star}
and (ii) (
4.49
) holds. Finally, we denote
\iota:=\min\{\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}\}
and consider arbitrary numbers
\iota^{\prime}\in(0,\iota)
and
\iota^{\prime\prime}\in(\iota^{\prime},\iota)
.

Now denoting again by
\Pi
the orthogonal projection onto
\mathcal{C}
(in the space
L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}(\mathcal{O})
), we introduce the notations

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}

\displaystyle:=\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}(I-\Pi)=(I-\Pi)\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}=:{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]},

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(I-\Pi),

\displaystyle{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}

\displaystyle:=(I-\Pi)\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}.

It is easy to observe that
\Pi\in\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega_{1}})
for every
\omega_{1}\in\mathcal{W}_{\mu^{-1/2}}
, and therefore (
4.48
) also implies

(4.50)

\|{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega_{1}})}+\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega_{1}})}\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\lambda}(t-s),\quad\forall\omega_{1}\in\left\{\omega,\omega_{\star}\right\}.

(Step 2: The Duhamel formula and iteration.) For given two-parameter families of operators
(\mathcal{U}_{1}(t,s))_{0\leq s\leq t}
and
(\mathcal{U}_{2}(t,s))_{0\leq s\leq t}
, we define

\mathcal{U}_{1}\star\mathcal{U}_{2}(t,s):=\int_{s}^{t}\mathcal{U}_{1}(t,\Theta)\mathcal{U}_{2}(\Theta,s)\,\textnormal{d}\Theta.
