In addition, for a two-parameter family of operators
(\mathcal{U}(t,s))_{0\leq s\leq t}
and a one-parameter family of operators
(\mathsf{G}(t))_{t\geq 0}
, we define the multiplication operation

\displaystyle(\mathcal{U}\mathsf{G})(t,s):=\mathcal{U}(t,s)\mathsf{G}(s),\quad(\mathsf{G}\mathcal{U})(t,s):=\mathsf{G}(t)\mathcal{U}(t,s).

Then Duhamel’s formula provides that

(4.51)

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}&=\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}+(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])\star\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}\\
&={}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}+\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}\star(\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}).\end{split}

Iterating (
4.51
), we obtain for every
n\in\mathbb{N}
that

(4.52)

\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}=\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star i}\star\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}+(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star n}\star\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp},

where we denoted the
n
-fold convolution as
(\mathcal{U})^{\star n}:=\mathcal{U}\star\cdots\star\mathcal{U}
. We also have the alternate expression from (
4.51
), for every
m\in\mathbb{N}

(4.53)

\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}={}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}+\sum_{i=1}^{m-1}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\star(\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma[k]}\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]})^{\star i}+\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}\star(\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]})^{\star m}.

Putting (
4.52
)–(
4.53
) together with the choice of
m=2
(which is enough for our purposes), we obtain the Dyson-like series

(4.54)

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}&=\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star i}\star\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}+(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star n}\star{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\\
&\quad+(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star n}\star{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}}[k]\star(\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}}[k])\\
&\quad+(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star n}\star\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}\star(\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]})^{\star 2}.\end{split}

(Step 3:
L^{\infty}_{\omega}\to L^{\infty}_{\omega}
estimates.) From (
4.54
) it obviously holds

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega})}+\sum_{i=1}^{n-1}\left\|(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star i}\star\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega})}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star n}\star{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega})}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star n}\star{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}}[k]\star(\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}}[k])\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega})}

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star n}\star\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}\star(\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]})^{\star 2}\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega})}

\displaystyle=:I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}+I_{5}.

Now beginning with
I_{1}
, thanks to (
4.48
) it is immediate that

I_{1}\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(t-s).

For
I_{2},I_{3},I_{4}
, we claim

Lemma 4.19
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
4.18
, there holds

I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota^{\prime}}(t-s).

Proof
. We begin by recalling the mere definition

(4.55)

\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]=\mathsf{P}_{\varsigma}[k]+M\psi_{R}(v),

where
\mathsf{P}_{\varsigma}[k]
is the projection part as written in (
4.6
). On the one hand, thanks to
\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\ll 1
, the very definition of
\mathcal{W}
, and using the fact that
L^{\infty}_{\omega}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\hookrightarrow L^{1}_{\langle v\rangle^{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we can easily establish that

\|\mathsf{P}_{\varsigma}[k]\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega})}\lesssim 1.

This inequality holds up to a constant that is independent of
\varsigma
, thanks to the fact that
|{\bf A}_{\varsigma}[k]|\lesssim\|k\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}
. On the other hand, having now fixed
M,R
sufficiently large,
M\psi_{R}
is nothing but a compactly supported and bounded function, and so

(4.56)

\|\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega})}\lesssim 1.

Using (
4.56
), we may therefore deduce that for every
i\in\mathbb{N}
there holds

\displaystyle\left\|(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star i}\star\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}(t,s)\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{s}^{t}\left\|(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star i}(t,\Theta)\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega})}\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}(\Theta,s)\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega})}\,\textnormal{d}\Theta

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{s}^{t}\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(t-\Theta)\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(\Theta-s)\,\textnormal{d}\Theta

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota^{\prime}}(t-s),

by utilizing (
4.50
). Hence

I_{2}\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota^{\prime}}(t-s).

In basically the same way, we obtain

I_{3}+I_{4}\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota^{\prime}}(t-s).

∎

At last, we consider
I_{5}
. We begin by listing some more basic estimates for the operator
\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]
:

Lemma 4.20
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
4.18
, the operator
\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]
satisfies

(4.57)

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega},L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}})}\lesssim 1,

(4.58)

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}},L^{\infty}_{\omega})}\lesssim 1,

(4.59)

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}})}\lesssim 1,

(4.60)

\displaystyle\|\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}})}\lesssim 1,,

up to some constants independent of time and
\varsigma
.

Proof
. Recalling (
4.55
), we note that obviously
M\psi_{R}\in\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega},L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}})
, whereas it holds
\|\mathsf{P}_{\varsigma}[k]\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega},L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}})}\lesssim 1
independently of
\varsigma
because
|{\bf A}_{\varsigma}[k]|\leq C\|k\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}^{2}
. Consequently (
4.57
) holds.

For (
4.58
), we use the very definition of
\mathcal{W}
, and in particular observe that

\langle v\rangle^{2}\mu\omega\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Using also the fact that (since
\omega_{\star}
is exponential)

|\rho[h]|+|j[h]|+|E[h]|\lesssim\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega\star}},

we can straightforwardly prove that the projection part of the operator
\mathsf{P}_{\varsigma}[k]
in (
4.6
) satisfies

\|\mathsf{P}_{\varsigma}[k]\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}},L^{\infty}_{\omega})}\lesssim 1.

Since
M\psi_{R}
merely truncates the support, we also clearly have

\forall h\in L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}},\quad|M\psi_{R}h\omega|\lesssim|h\omega_{\star}|\mathbf{1}_{|v|\leq 2R}\frac{\omega}{\omega_{\star}}\lesssim_{R}\|h\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}}}.

Thus, we conclude to

\|\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}},L^{\infty}_{\omega})}\lesssim 1,

proving (
4.58
). The proof of (
4.59
) is almost the same and straightforward.

At last, the estimate (
4.60
) also follows similarly. For instance, looking at just the very last term of
\mathsf{P}_{\varsigma}[k]h
in (
4.6
), we can compute

\displaystyle\|(1+\rho[k])E[h](|v|^{2}-d)\mu\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\iint_{\mathcal{O}}|E[h]|^{2}\langle v\rangle^{4}\mu\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v

\displaystyle=\int_{\Omega}|E[h]|^{2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\langle v\rangle^{4}\mu\,\textnormal{d}v\right)\textnormal{d}x

\displaystyle=C\int_{\Omega}|E[h]|^{2}\,\textnormal{d}x

\displaystyle\lesssim\|h\|_{L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}}}^{2},

the last line following by Cauchy–Schwarz. All of the other terms in (
4.6
) can be handled in the same way, and we obtain that

\|\mathsf{P}_{\varsigma}[k]\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}})}\lesssim 1.

We easily conclude to (
4.60
) and this completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

Now, we break down the convolution in
I_{5}
into

(4.61)

\left\|(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star n}\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}},L^{\infty}_{\omega})},\quad\left\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}})},\quad\left\|(\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]})^{\star 2}\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega},L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}})}

and look at each portion separately. Starting from the right, we put (
4.57
) and (
4.48
) together to deduce

\displaystyle\left\|\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega},L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(t-s).

However we can also put together (
4.56
) with (
4.48
), and thus

\left\|\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega})}\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(t-s).

In conclusion,

(4.62)

\begin{split}\left\|(\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]})^{\star 2}\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega},L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}})}&\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota^{\prime\prime}}(t-s).\end{split}

For the middle term in (
4.61
), we just use the bound in (
4.47
):

(4.63)

\left\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}^{\perp}\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}})}\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\lambda}(t-s)\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(t-s).

For the remaining left term in (
4.61
),
in the spirit of
[
11
,
23
,
39
]
we shall prove that there exists
n\in\mathbb{N}
large enough so that

(4.64)

\left\|(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star n}\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}},L^{\infty}_{\omega})}\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota^{\prime\prime}}(t-s).

Toward this end, we can first write (using that
\|\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega})}\lesssim 1
)

(4.65)

\begin{split}&\left\|(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star n}\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}},L^{\infty}_{\omega})}\\
&\lesssim\left\|(\mathsf{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])\star(\mathsf{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star(n-2)}\star\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}},L^{\infty}_{\omega})}\\
&\lesssim\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{\Theta}\left\|(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])(t,\Theta)\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}},L^{\infty}_{\omega})}\left\|(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star(n-2)}(\Theta,\Theta^{\prime})\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}},L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}})}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\times\left\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(\Theta^{\prime},s)\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}})}\,\textnormal{d}\Theta^{\prime}\textnormal{d}\Theta.\end{split}

Thanks to (
4.58
) and (
4.48
), the first term on the right-hand side of (
4.65
) may be bounded as

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])(t,\Theta)\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}},L^{\infty}_{\omega})}\lesssim\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,\Theta)\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega})}\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(t-\Theta).

For the second term on the right-hand side of (
4.65
), we claim that:

Lemma 4.21
.

There exists
n\in\mathbb{N}
large enough so that

(4.66)

\left\|(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star(n-2)}\star\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}},L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}})}\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota^{\prime\prime}}(t-s).

Proof
. Let us begin with an estimate for the case
n=3
. We write

(4.67)

\begin{split}&(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])\star\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}}[k]\\
&=\int_{s}^{\frac{t+s}{2}}\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,\Theta)\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k](\Theta)\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(\Theta,s)\,\textnormal{d}\Theta+\int_{\frac{t+s}{2}}^{t}\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,\Theta)\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k](\Theta)\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(\Theta,s)\,\textnormal{d}\Theta,\end{split}

in order to isolate the possibly non-integrable factor arising in the ultracontractive estimate (
4.49
). For the first integral here, we may estimate

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{s}^{\frac{t+s}{2}}\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,\Theta)\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k](\Theta)\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(\Theta,s)\,\textnormal{d}\Theta\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}},L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{s}^{\frac{t+s}{2}}\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,\Theta)\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}},L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}})}\|\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k](\Theta)\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}})}\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(\Theta,s)\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}})}\,\textnormal{d}\Theta

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{s}^{\frac{t+s}{2}}\mathfrak{q}(t-\Theta)\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(\Theta-s)\,\textnormal{d}\Theta

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(t-s)\int_{s}^{\frac{t+s}{2}}\max\{(t-\Theta)^{-\vartheta},1\}\,\textnormal{d}\Theta.

If
|t-s|\leq 2
then

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{\frac{t+s}{2}}\max\{(t-\Theta)^{-\vartheta},1\}\,\textnormal{d}\Theta

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t-1}\frac{1}{(t-\Theta)^{\vartheta}}\,\textnormal{d}\Theta+1

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\vartheta-1}\left(\frac{1}{(t-s)^{\vartheta-1}}-1\right)+1

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{(t-s)^{\vartheta-1}}+1,

whereas if
|t-s|>2
then

\int_{s}^{\frac{t+s}{2}}\max\{(t-\Theta)^{-\vartheta},1\}\,\textnormal{d}\Theta=\int_{s}^{\frac{t+s}{2}}\,\textnormal{d}\Theta=\frac{t-s}{2},

and therefore we conclude to

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{s}^{\frac{t+s}{2}}\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,\Theta)\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k](\Theta)\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(\Theta,s)\,\textnormal{d}\Theta\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}},L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(t-s)\left(1+\max\{(t-s)^{-(\vartheta-1)},2\}\right)\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(t-s)\left(1+\max\{(t-s)^{-(\vartheta-1)},1\}\right).

For the second integral in (
4.67
), we reverse the order to write (using (
4.59
))

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{\frac{t+s}{2}}^{t}\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,\Theta)\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k](\Theta)\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(\Theta,s)\,\textnormal{d}\Theta\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}},L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\frac{t+s}{2}}^{t}\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,\Theta)\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}})}\|\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k](\Theta)\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}})}\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(\Theta,s)\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}},L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}})}\,\textnormal{d}\Theta

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\frac{t+s}{2}}^{t}\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(t-\Theta)\mathfrak{q}(\Theta-s)\,\textnormal{d}\Theta

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(t-s)\int_{\frac{t+s}{2}}^{t}\max\{(\Theta-s)^{-\vartheta},1\}\,\textnormal{d}\Theta,

and the same argument provides

\displaystyle\left\|\int_{\frac{t+s}{2}}^{t}\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,\Theta)\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k](\Theta)\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(\Theta,s)\,\textnormal{d}\Theta\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}},L^{\infty}_{\omega})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(t-s)\left(1+\max\{(t-s)^{-(\vartheta-1)},1\}\right).

In all we have shown that for the case
n=3
, there holds

(4.68)

\left\|(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])\star\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}},L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}})}\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(t-s)\left(1+\max\{(t-s)^{-(\vartheta-1)},1\}\right).

If
\vartheta-1>0
, we repeat this argument for the case
n=4
. We write similarly

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star 2}\star\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}},L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{s}^{\frac{t+s}{2}}\|(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])\star\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,\Theta)\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}},L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}})}\|(\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k]\,\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]})(\Theta,s)\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}})}\,\textnormal{d}\Theta

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\frac{t+s}{2}}^{t}\|(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])(t,\Theta)\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}},L^{\infty}_{\omega})}\|(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])\star\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}(\Theta,s)\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}},L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}})}\,\textnormal{d}\Theta

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{s}^{\frac{t+s}{2}}\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(t-\Theta)\left(1+\max\{(t-\Theta)^{-(\vartheta-1)},1\}\right)\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(\Theta-s)\,\textnormal{d}\Theta

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{\frac{t+s}{2}}^{t}\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(t-\Theta)\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(\Theta-s)\left(1+\max\{(\Theta-s)^{-(\vartheta-1)},1\}\right)\,\textnormal{d}\Theta

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(t-s)\int_{s}^{\frac{t+s}{2}}\left(1+\max\{(t-\Theta)^{-(\vartheta-1)},1\}\right)\textnormal{d}\Theta

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(t-s)\left(1+\max\{(t-s)^{-(\vartheta-2)},1\}\right),

by using (
4.68
), (
4.60
)–(
4.58
), and (
4.48
) with either
\omega_{1}=\mu^{-1/2}
or
\omega_{1}=\omega_{\star}
. Iterating until
\vartheta-(n-2)<0
, we deduce

\displaystyle\|(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star(n-2)}\star\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}},L^{\infty}_{\omega_{\star}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(t-s)\left(1+\max\{(t-s)^{-(\vartheta-(n-2))},1\}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota^{\prime\prime}}(t-s),

which proves (
4.66
).
∎

As a consequence of Lemma
4.21
, we may now prove (
4.64
). Indeed, returning to (
4.65
), we have

\displaystyle\left\|(\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{H}_{\varsigma}[k]}\mathsf{G}_{\varsigma}[k])^{\star n}\right\|_{\mathscr{B}(L^{2}_{\mu^{-1/2}},L^{\infty}_{\omega})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{\Theta}\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(t-\Theta)\mathfrak{p}_{\iota^{\prime\prime}}(\Theta-\Theta^{\prime})\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(\Theta^{\prime}-s)\,\textnormal{d}\Theta^{\prime}\textnormal{d}\Theta

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota^{\prime\prime}}(t-s),

thanks to (
4.48
)–(
4.58
) and Lemma
4.21
. At last, gathering (
4.62
)–(
4.63
)–(
4.64
) together, we have obtained all the estimates required for the three terms in (
4.61
), and namely we conclude to

I_{5}\lesssim\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{\Theta}\mathfrak{p}_{\iota^{\prime\prime}}(t-\Theta)\mathfrak{p}_{\iota}(\Theta-\Theta^{\prime})\mathfrak{p}_{\iota^{\prime\prime}}(\Theta^{\prime}-s)\,\textnormal{d}\Theta^{\prime}\textnormal{d}\Theta\lesssim(t-s)^{2}\mathfrak{p}_{\iota^{\prime\prime}}(t-s)\lesssim\mathfrak{p}_{\iota^{\prime}}(t-s).

We collect the estimates for all of the
I_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq 5
to complete the proof.

4.8.
Back to the nonlinear problem

Our business with the linear problem is now done, and so we now aim to return to the nonlinear problem. We remind the reader that the operator
{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]
is defined in (
4.3
) as

\displaystyle{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]h

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\left(E[k]-{\bf A}_{\varsigma}[k]\right)\Delta_{v}h+(v\rho[k]-j[k])\cdot\nabla_{v}h+d\rho[k]h

\displaystyle\quad-(\rho[h]j[k]-\rho[k]j[h])\cdot v\mu

\displaystyle\quad-\left((E[k]-{\bf A}_{\varsigma}[k])\rho[h]-\frac{1}{d}\rho[k]E[h]+\frac{1}{d}j[k]\cdot j[h]\right)(|v|^{2}-d)\mu.

Proposition 4.22
.

We assume that
c\leq c_{\star}
. There exists some
a_{0}>0
small enough such that for every
h_{0}\in\mathcal{C}
satisfying
\|h_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\leq a_{0}
, there exists a corresponding weak and renormalized solution
h^{\varsigma}\in\mathcal{X}_{\omega,c}\cap\mathcal{C}
to the nonlinear PDE

(4.69)

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}h^{\varsigma}=-v\cdot\nabla_{x}h^{\varsigma}+\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}h^{\varsigma}+{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[h^{\varsigma}]h^{\varsigma},\\
h^{\varsigma}|_{t=0}=h_{0},\\
\gamma_{-}h^{\varsigma}(t,x,v)=\gamma_{+}h^{\varsigma}(t,x,\mathscr{V}_{x}).\end{cases}

This one satisfies the decay estimate

\|h^{\varsigma}_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}(\mathcal{O})}\leq C^{\prime}e^{-\iota^{\prime}t}\|h_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}

for some constants
C^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}>0
which are all independent of
\varsigma
.

Proof
. For each
k\in\mathcal{X}_{\omega,c}
with
c\leq c_{\star}
, we define the map
\Psi:\mathcal{X}_{\omega,c}\to L^{\infty}_{\omega}
via

\Psi:k\mapsto\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,0)h_{0},

so that
\Psi(k)
is the solution to the linear problem

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}h=-v\cdot\nabla_{x}h+\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}h+{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[k]h,\\
h|_{t=0}=h_{0},\\
\gamma_{-}h(t,x,v)=\gamma_{+}h(t,x,\mathscr{V}_{x}).\end{cases}

Since we can always embed
L^{\infty}_{\omega}\hookrightarrow L^{2}_{\widetilde{\omega}}
for some
\widetilde{\omega}
growing slowly enough, Proposition
4.11
tells us that
\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,0)h_{0}
is the unique solution to this equation which corresponds to initial datum
h_{0}
. Also, according to Proposition
4.18
, we know that

(4.70)

\|\mathcal{U}_{\mathsf{F}_{\varsigma}[k]}(t,0)h_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\leq C^{\prime}e^{-\iota^{\prime}(t-s)}\|h_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}

for
C^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}>0
independent of
\varsigma
. Therefore,
\Psi
is well-defined. Furthermore, if

\|h_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\leq a_{0}:=\frac{c}{C^{\prime}},

then (
4.70
) tells us that
\Psi
leaves
\mathcal{X}_{\omega,c}
invariant. Hence, endowing
\mathcal{X}_{\omega,c}
with the
L^{\infty}_{\omega}-*
topology, we just need to prove that
\Psi:\mathcal{X}_{\omega,c}\to\mathcal{X}_{\omega,c}
is continuous in order to prove the existence of a fixed point of
\Psi
. The required arguments are completely the same as with Proposition
3.8
and thus skipped. We just point out that the convolutions in the definition of
{\bf A}_{\varsigma}[\cdot]
allow us to establish strong stability of this term, even if we only know that its argument converges weakly.
∎

Proposition 4.23
.

We assume that
h_{0}\in\mathcal{C}
and that
\|h_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}\leq a_{0}
, with
a_{0}
chosen as in Proposition
4.22
. Denote by
(h^{\varsigma})_{\varsigma>0}\subset\mathcal{X}_{\omega,c}\cap\mathcal{C}
a family of solutions to (
4.69
) (again as constructed in Proposition
4.22
). Then as
\varsigma\searrow 0
there is a weak
-*
limit point
h\in\mathcal{X}_{\omega,c}\cap\mathcal{C}\cap L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x,v}\cap L^{2}_{t,x}H^{1}_{v}
which is a weak and renormalized solution to the nonlinear problem

(4.71)

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}h=-v\cdot\nabla_{x}h+\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}h+\Upsilon(h),\\
h|_{t=0}=h_{0},\\
\gamma_{-}h(t,x,v)=\gamma_{+}h(t,x,\mathscr{V}_{x}v).\end{cases}

This one satisfies also the estimate

\|h_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}(\mathcal{O})}\leq C^{\prime}e^{-\iota^{\prime}t}\|h_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}(\mathcal{O})}.

Proof
. The proof is a repetition of the one provided in Proposition
3.9
, and we therefore only sketch it. We define the weighted space

\mathcal{Y}:=\left\{h:\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}\;\middle|\;\left\|e^{\iota^{\prime}t}h\omega\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq C^{\prime}\right\}.

Thanks to Proposition
4.22
, we know that
(h^{\varsigma})
is bounded in
\mathcal{Y}
, and thus the Banach–Alaoglu theorem provides a limit
\mathfrak{h}
satisfying

\begin{split}&e^{\iota^{\prime}t}h\omega\rightharpoonup\mathfrak{h}\quad\text{in }L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{d})-*,\\
&\|\mathfrak{h}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\Omega\times\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq C^{\prime}\|h_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}.\end{split}

Defining now
h:=e^{-\iota^{\prime}t}\omega^{-1}\mathfrak{h}
, we obtain that (at least)
h^{\varsigma}\rightharpoonup h
in the sense of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\overline{\mathcal{O}})
and
\|h_{t}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}(\mathcal{O})}\leq C^{\prime}e^{-\iota^{\prime}t}\|h_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}
.

From the equation (
4.69
), we observe that it is enough to check whether we can pass to the limit of the term
{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[h^{\varsigma}]h^{\varsigma}
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}((0,\infty)\times\mathcal{O})
. Indeed, the linear terms can be passed to the limit easily; after that, the renormalization properties, traces, and boundary condition can be recovered in the same way as in the proof of Proposition
3.9
.

Towards the end of analyzing the limit of
{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[h^{\varsigma}]h^{\varsigma}
, we write its explicit form here

(4.72)

\begin{split}{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[h^{\varsigma}]h^{\varsigma}&=\frac{1}{d}(E[h^{\varsigma}]-{\bf A}_{\varsigma}[h^{\varsigma}])\Delta_{v}h^{\varsigma}+(v\rho[h]-j[h])\cdot\nabla_{v}h^{\varsigma}+d\rho[h^{\varsigma}]h^{\varsigma}\\
&\quad-\frac{1}{d}\left((E[h^{\varsigma}]-{\bf A}_{\varsigma}[h^{\varsigma}])\rho[h^{\varsigma}]-\rho[h^{\varsigma}]E[h^{\varsigma}]+|j[h^{\varsigma}]|^{2}\right)(|v|^{2}-d)\mu.\end{split}

First, we remark that again using
L^{\infty}_{\omega}\hookrightarrow L^{2}
, and resorting once more to hypoelliptic estimates, we may assume that
h^{\varsigma}\to h
in
L^{2}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathcal{O})
and a.e., as well as
\nabla_{v}h^{\varsigma}\rightharpoonup\nabla_{v}h
weakly in
L^{2}_{t,x,v}
(see the proof of Proposition
3.9
). Since
\rho[\cdot],j[\cdot],E[\cdot]
are linear in their arguments, it follows that

(4.73)

\rho[h^{\varsigma}]\to\rho[h],\quad j[h^{\varsigma}]\to j[h],\quad E[h^{\varsigma}]\to E[h]\quad\text{a.e. in }\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\Omega.

On the other hand, since
|\rho[h^{\varsigma}]|,|j[h^{\varsigma}]|,|E[h^{\varsigma}]|\lesssim\|h^{\varsigma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathcal{O})}\lesssim 1
, we deduce by Vitali’s theorem that the convergences in (
4.73
) hold in
L^{p}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\overline{\Omega})
for every
p\in[1,\infty)
. We also know that

0\leq{\bf A}_{\varsigma}[h^{\varsigma}]\leq\frac{\|j[h^{\varsigma}]*_{t}{\bf s}_{\varsigma}\star_{\varsigma}{\bf r}_{\varsigma}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\Omega)}^{2}}{1-\|\rho[k]*_{t}{\bf s}_{\varsigma}\star_{\varsigma}{\bf r}_{\varsigma}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\Omega)}}\leq\frac{\|j[h^{\varsigma}]\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\Omega)}^{2}}{1-\|\rho[k]\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\Omega)}}\lesssim\|h^{\varsigma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\omega}}^{2}\lesssim 1,

and therefore

{\bf A}_{\varsigma}[h^{\varepsilon}]\to{\bf A}[h]:=\frac{|j[h]|^{2}}{1+\rho[k]}\quad\text{a.e. and in }L^{p}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\overline{\Omega}),\quad\forall p\in[1,\infty).

In similar manner,

|j[h^{\varsigma}]|^{2}\to|j[h]|^{2}\quad\text{a.e. and in }L^{p}_{\rm loc}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\overline{\Omega}).

Therefore there is no problem with passing to the limit of
{}^{\perp}\!\!\!\>\Upsilon_{\varsigma}[h^{\varsigma}]h^{\varsigma}
in the sense of
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}((0,\infty)\times\mathcal{O})
. We note in particular that the last line of (
4.72
) converges to

\begin{split}&-\frac{1}{d}\left((E[h^{\varsigma}]-{\bf A}_{\varsigma}[h^{\varsigma}])\rho[h^{\varsigma}]-\rho[h^{\varsigma}]E[h^{\varsigma}]+|j[h^{\varsigma}]|^{2}\right)(|v|^{2}-d)\mu\\
&\to-\frac{1}{d}\left(\left(E[h]-\frac{|j[h]|^{2}}{1+\rho[h]}\right)\rho[h]-\rho[h]E[h]+|j[h]|^{2}\right)(|v|^{2}-d)\mu\\
&=-\frac{1}{d}\frac{|j[h]|^{2}}{1+\rho[h]}(|v|^{2}-d)\mu,\end{split}

and so we have established that
h
solves (
4.71
) in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}((0,\infty)\times\mathcal{O})
with
\Upsilon(h)
as given in (
4.1
).

Finally, one can check that
h\in\mathcal{C}
as follows: we note that, owing to the embedding
L^{\infty}_{\omega}\hookrightarrow L^{1}_{\langle v\rangle^{2}}
, we can admit for any
R\in\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}
the functions
1,R(x)\cdot v,|v|^{2}
as test functions in the Green’s formula for
h
via a standard approximation procedure. For any of these test functions, the contribution from the boundary vanishes, and we can deduce from these Green’s formulae that

t\mapsto\iint_{\mathcal{O}}h_{t}(1,R(x)\cdot v,|v|^{2})\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v

is absolutely continuous for every
R\in\mathcal{R}_{\Omega}
. Thanks to
h\in L^{\infty}_{\omega}
we may apply the Gauss–Ostrogradsky theorem freely to deduce, after some straightforward computations, that

\frac{\textnormal{d}}{\textnormal{d}t}\iint_{\mathcal{O}}h_{t}(1,R(x)\cdot v,|v|^{2})\,\textnormal{d}x\textnormal{d}v=0

and we therefore obtain that
h_{t}\in\mathcal{C}
because
h_{0}\in\mathcal{C}
.
∎

4.9.
Proof of Theorem
III

The proof here is a repetition of that of Theorem
II
. Writing
f:=\mu+h
where
h
is as in Proposition
4.23
, it is clear that (i)
f\in L^{\infty}_{\omega}\cap L^{\infty}_{t}L^{2}_{x,v}\cap L^{2}_{t,x}H^{1}_{v}
(recalling that
L^{\infty}_{\omega}\hookrightarrow L^{2}
), (ii)
f
satisfies the compatibility condition (C) (because
h\in\mathcal{C}
), and (iii)
f
is a solution to (
1.1
) in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}([0,t]\times\overline{\mathcal{O}})
for every
t>0
. We can then check, by repeating the proof of Proposition
3.9
, that
f
is a renormalized solution to (
1.1
); finally, the decay (
1.8
) is an immediate consequence of Proposition
4.23
.

5.

The author thanks Young-Pil Choi for his advice and support. This work received funding from NRF grants no. 2022R1A2C1002820 and RS-2024-00406821.

Appendix A
Formal computation of the linearized operator

Let
\mathscr{F}
denote the nonlinear Fokker–Planck operator in (
1.1
). In this section, we compute the linearization of
\mathscr{F}
based at the point
\mu
, and explain why
\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}
takes its form. In other words, we wish to find the following first order approximation of
\mathscr{F}
:

\mathscr{F}[F]\approx\mathscr{F}[\mu]+D\mathscr{F}[\mu](F-\mu)=D\mathscr{F}[\mu](F-\mu)=:\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}(F-\mu).

In the above, we denoted by
D\mathscr{F}
the Gateaux derivative, and we noted that
\mathscr{F}[\mu]=0
.

Toward this end, we write

\displaystyle G=\mu+\varepsilon g,

and aim to collect all terms that are formally up to first order in
\varepsilon
. In this way, a straightforward computation allows us to check that

\displaystyle\rho[G]

\displaystyle=1+\varepsilon\rho[g],

\displaystyle u[G]

\displaystyle=\frac{\varepsilon j[g]}{1+\varepsilon\rho[g]},\quad j[g]:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}vf\,\textnormal{d}v.

Using the expansion
\frac{1}{1+x}=1-x+x^{2}-\cdots
, we find

u[G]=\varepsilon j[g](1-\varepsilon\rho[g]+o(\varepsilon))=\varepsilon j[g]+(\textnormal{$\varepsilon$-higher order terms}).

Similarly, setting
E[g]:=\int|v|^{2}g\textnormal{d}v
, we can find

E[G]=d+\varepsilon E[g],

so that

\begin{split}\theta[G]&:=\frac{1}{d\rho[G]}\Big(E[G]-\rho[G]|u[G]|^{2}\Big)\\
&=\frac{1}{d(1+\varepsilon\rho[g])}\left(d+\varepsilon E[g]-(1+\varepsilon\rho[g])\left|\frac{\varepsilon j[g]}{1+\varepsilon\rho[g]}\right|^{2}\right)\\
&=\frac{1+\varepsilon E[g]/d}{1+\varepsilon\rho[g]}+o(\varepsilon),\end{split}

and again using the expansion of
\frac{1}{1+x}
, we get

\theta[G]=1+\frac{\varepsilon}{d}E[g]-\varepsilon\rho[g]+(\textnormal{$\varepsilon$-higher order terms}).

That is, up to first order in
\varepsilon
:

\theta[G]=1+\frac{\varepsilon}{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(|v|^{2}-d)g\,\textnormal{d}v.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathscr{F}[\mu+\varepsilon g]

\displaystyle=\mathscr{F}[G]

\displaystyle=\rho[G](\theta[G]\Delta_{v}G+\nabla_{v}\cdot((v-u[G])G))

\displaystyle\approx(1+\varepsilon\rho[g])\left(\left(1+\frac{\varepsilon}{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(|v|^{2}-d)g\,\textnormal{d}v\right)(\Delta_{v}\mu+\varepsilon\Delta_{v}g)\right)

\displaystyle\quad+(1+\varepsilon\rho[g])\left(d(\mu+\varepsilon g)+\left(v-\varepsilon j[g]\right)\cdot\nabla_{v}(\mu+\varepsilon g)\right)

\displaystyle\approx\underbrace{\Delta_{v}\mu+d\mu+v\cdot\nabla_{v}\mu}_{=0}

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon\rho[g](\underbrace{\Delta_{v}\mu+d\mu+v\cdot\nabla_{v}\mu}_{=0})

\displaystyle\quad+\varepsilon\left[(\Delta_{v}g+\nabla_{v}\cdot(vg))-j[g]\cdot\nabla_{v}\mu+\left(\frac{1}{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(|v|^{2}-d)g\,\textnormal{d}v\right)\Delta_{v}\mu\right]

\displaystyle\quad+(\textnormal{$\varepsilon$-higher order terms})

\displaystyle\approx\varepsilon\left[\Delta_{v}g+\nabla_{v}\cdot(vg)+j[f]\cdot(v\mu)+\left(\frac{1}{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(|w|^{2}-d)g\,\textnormal{d}w\right)(|v|^{2}-d)\mu\right].

Hence, using that
\mathscr{F}[\mu]=0
:

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{\mathscr{F}[\mu+\varepsilon g]-\mathscr{F}[\mu]}{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\Delta_{v}g+\nabla_{v}\cdot(vg)}_{=\mathsf{C}_{\rm FP}g}+\underbrace{j[g]\cdot(v\mu)+\left(\frac{1}{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(|w|^{2}-d)g\,\textnormal{d}w\right)(|v|^{2}-d)\mu}_{=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\pi_{i}g+2\pi_{d+1}g}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{C}_{\rm lin}g.
